3,700 Miles: TAP Air Portugal Unveils New Unusual Longest A321LR Route

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

TAP Air Portugal is planning a significant change to its Porto–Luanda service, deploying the Airbus A321LR on the 3,737-mile (6,014 km) route from October 31, 2027. The move will create the airline's longest scheduled nonstop A321LR flight, narrowly surpassing its existing Lisbon–Belém service.

The decision is particularly noteworthy because TAP intends to replace its 298-seat Airbus A330-900 with a 171-seat narrowbody, reducing passenger capacity by approximately 43%. The long sector also raises questions about baggage and payload limitations, particularly given the substantial baggage requirements that can characterize routes between Europe and sub-Saharan Africa. The change remains provisional, with almost a year until its planned introduction.

According to AeroRoutes, TAP's preliminary winter 2027/28 schedule replaces the A330-900 with the A321LR on its twice-weekly service from Porto to Luanda. The route connects northern Portugal with Angola's new Dr. António Agostinho Neto International Airport, which has progressively replaced the city's older airport. The proposed flight schedule is detailed below.

The aircraft substitution will reduce weekly capacity from 1,192 to 684 seats across both directions, a reduction of 508 seats. TAP's A321LR configuration accommodates 16 business-class passengers and 155 economy passengers, compared with 34 business and 264 economy seats on its A330-900s. The smaller aircraft could allow TAP to better match capacity with demand while maintaining nonstop service. When TAP first received the A321LR, then-CEO Antonoaldo Neves explained its strategic importance:

“With its superior range, we can comfortably explore new markets in North America, South America and Africa from Portugal, and offer nonstop flights with the right size of aircraft.”

However, the long flight raises operational questions. Airbus advertises an A321LR range of approximately 4,000 nautical miles, equivalent to 4,603 miles (7,408 km), comfortably exceeding Porto–Luanda's great-circle distance of 3,737 miles (6,014 km) on paper. Nevertheless, the aircraft's range depends on payload and operating conditions.

Heavy checked baggage, commercial cargo, headwinds, and fuel reserves could all influence its performance. TAP has not announced any passenger or baggage restrictions, and whether either will be necessary remains unknown.

The Porto–Luanda deployment will surpass TAP's Lisbon–Belém service by just 11 miles (18 km). The Portuguese flag carrier has increasingly used its A321LRs to connect smaller long-haul markets across the Atlantic, particularly in northeastern Brazil and North America.

Its introduction of the aircraft transformed TAP's transatlantic network, allowing the airline to serve destinations previously impossible to operate economically because they would have required larger widebodies to cover the distance. TAP's planned 2027 A321LR network includes 16 nonstop routes exceeding 2,000 miles (3,219 km).

Six of the ten longest connect Portugal with the Americas, three serve Brazil specifically, and the new Porto–Luanda service is the only African route on the list. The ranking illustrates the versatility of an aircraft that TAP originally introduced to complement its A330-900 fleet. Crucially, the record applies only to nonstop flights: TAP has operated longer one-stop A321LR services, including Lisbon–Belém–Manaus, covering a combined 4,534 miles (7,297 km).

Introduced in November 2024, the three-weekly service was scheduled to take 11 hours and 45 minutes, including its stop in Belém. TAP has also just launched a triangular Lisbon–São Luís–Fortaleza–Lisbon service, demonstrating how intermediate stops extend the reach of its long-haul narrowbody fleet.

Simple Flying had the chance to check out this narrowbody flying over the Atlantic!

The destination is itself undergoing a major transformation. Dr. António Agostinho Neto International Airport (NBJ) was inaugurated in November 2023, approximately 25 miles (40 km) south-east of central Luanda, replacing Quatro de Fevereiro Airport (LAD) as the city's principal passenger gateway.

Angola's national carrier, TAAG Angola Airlines, transferred its international operations in October 2025, while the wider transition was completed in March 2026. The airport forms part of Angola's ambition to establish a major Southern African aviation hub. The approximately $3 billion facility is designed to handle 15 million passengers annually, supported by two runways and substantial cargo infrastructure.

TAAG Angola Airlines, TAP, Air France, Lufthansa, Ethiopian, Qatar Airways, Royal Air Maroc, Airlink

However, its initial traffic has been considerably lower than anticipated. Just over 750,000 passengers used the airport during 2025, although that figure reflects a transitional year before all scheduled airlines had relocated. Despite its ambitious scale, the airport faces challenges attracting and retaining international services.

Turkish Airlines removed Luanda from its network in 2026, following the withdrawal of Brussels Airlines last year. Nevertheless, TAP, Lufthansa, Air France, and several African carriers maintain connections. TAP's planned A321LR substitution illustrates how it is able to adjust capacity to demand and still maintain the nonstop service.

Despite promising a heady mix of Vampire Survivors and Warhammer fan-service, Warhammer Survivors is somehow less than the sum of its parts

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

You know my favourite thing about Vampire Survivors? The slow build of strength, the swelling power fantasy, the edging. Developer Poncle has proved itself incredibly adept at goading players into ever more ludicrous setups, demonstrating that with some patience, a canny eye for build-crafting, and some very rudimentary knowledge of your surroundings, even an old man armed with garlic can be an extinction level event for the amassing undead. Warhammer Survivors sadly never reaches those heights.

Created by Warhammer 40,000: Boltgun 2 studio Auroch Digital, Warhammer Survivors is - as its name suggests - an officially-licensed attempt to transplant developer Poncle's winning Vampire Survivors' formula to the Warhammer universe. Cue 40+ characters ripped from Warhammer lore getting the action-roguelike treatment, crossbows instead of clock lancets, rocket launchers instead of fire wands, bomb Squids instead of skeletons… you get the idea.

But this fusion doesn't really work. While Vampire Survivors was always a bit of an uphill battle from the start, it still hooked you with a thread that pulled you forward, tempting you with power-ups and other intriguing toys to play with en route. If hour one was a slog, hour five sparked curiosity, and hour 10 was a romp. In Warhammer Survivors, however, hour 10 feels like just as much of a slog as hour one. And even though Warhammer Survivors offers two alternate universes - the grimdark future of 40K and the old-school fantasy of Age of Sigmar, each a full game in its own right - this does little to alleviate the grind.

It's all so slow. Characters move slowly, upgrades come slowly, unlocking the mode that makes things move a bit faster comes slowly. Every inch feels like a mile, and the moreish power creep - tantalisingly propulsive in Vampire Survivors - is out of reach for so long per run here, it's discouraging. That's not to say there isn't any resolution to all the tension. Every now and then, you'll roll the right dice, appease the right gods, make the right moves. Your miniature pixel heroes will overcome the odds, and burn through the writhing mass of Orks, Skaven, Tyranids, Nurgle, or whatever other bastards are trying to get you down today.

The Survivors genre - sometimes known as 'bullet heaven' now - lives and dies on the strength of the over-powered nonsense that emerges as all your weapons and evolutions start to harmonise with each other in brutal synchronicity. Warhammer Survivors seems reluctant to let you get to this level, though. Even 30 hours in (!), some runs will end before they ever really get started - no matter how many skips, rerolls, or other chance-altering upgrades you've committed to your save file.

And because this is really two games glued into one, you have to do all the requisite grinding and levelling on both sides. With massive maps and impossibly slow character movement (especially on some of the bulkier characters), collecting all the items per location is more of a chore than it is fun. This wouldn't be so bad if these items were just curios, but in most cases they're pretty significant quality-of-life enhancers. Why are you hiding them from me, Auroch!?

It's almost impressive how Warhammer Survivors manages to feel like a step back from Poncle's original in many ways: yes, there's more content, but there appears to be a lack of understanding of why this genre immediately took off and spawned a myriad of imitators. The very worst weapons are the only ones available at the beginning, so unlocking new characters and armaments is necessary rather than a fun bonus. Lore is paper thin - in Vampire Survivors, we had a blank canvas and the lack of lore didn't really matter, but Warhammer is steeped in its own mythos, so relegating it to merely set dressing feels remiss. Some of the synergies between items and their evolutions don't really make sense. The maps are awkward and fussy to navigate. The locked requirements to take on stage-end bosses are even more cryptic than in Vampire Survivors. In almost all the ways that matter when it comes to fun, Warhammer Survivors pales in comparison to its forebear. This wouldn't be so egregious if the game wasn't trying to emulate it quite so completely, but it is - and as such, all the actually decent design choices hidden behind layers of frustration are marred.

As a Warhammer and Survivors fan, I've found myself floating back to the game, even if - sometimes - my eyes roll back into my head within minutes of my first run. But it's not a complete disaster: on those occasions where the stars do align, Warhammer Survivors finds its way to a power fantasy worthy of both franchises. You'll grab a Flamer early doors, then pick up the passive Glue to reduce incoming damage. You'll gain the Heavy Flamer, the Boltgun, and the projectile-multiplying Dice Cube, eventually unlocking the Heavy Bolter - slicing through enemies and all but melting the face off that pesky Prophet of Gork.

I just wish there wasn't so much friction in actually getting to that point. I imagine Warhammer Survivors is the sort of game I'll keep on my harddrive (and on multiple devices; it costs a fiver and it's on Game Pass) for a while. If you can endure the teething pains and engage with the wobblier systems, there's the usual Survivors payoff waiting for you as the screen fills with pixelated blood and countless helpless enemies fall to your blades. Maybe the stress of the early game even makes this catharsis even better. So even with its qualms, Warhammer Survivors is still an enjoyable, lo-fi stress reliever to snack on in between other, meatier games.

Android, Nintendo Switch, Nintendo Switch 2, PC, PS5, Xbox One, Xbox Series X/S, iOS

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Anthropic Sets IPO Price Range at $70 to $78, Valuing Company at Up to $1.4 Trillion

Anthropic has set a price range of $70 to $78 per share for its initial public offering, according to an updated filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Tuesday.

The AI company plans to sell 825 million shares of Class A common stock, 801.4 million of them new shares and 23.6 million from existing shareholders, on the Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," which it revealed when it made its IPO filing public last month. At the top of the range, the offering would raise about $64.4 billion, including the selling shareholders' stake. Underwriters have an option to buy a further 123.75 million shares.

Anthropic expects to price the offering on November 18 and begin trading on November 19, and is targeting a retail allocation of about 20%, well above the norm for an IPO, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the details.

Based on the roughly 13.09 billion shares the filing says will be outstanding after the offering, the range implies a market value of about $916 billion to $1.02 trillion. On a fully diluted basis, the company could be worth up to $1.405 trillion according to CNBC, up from the $965 billion valuation it announced in a funding round in May.

Alongside the IPO, seven of the companies that supply Anthropic's computing power have agreed to buy a combined $26 billion of Class N common stock, which has no voting rights, at the IPO price. At the midpoint of the range, Google and Nvidia would each buy about $10 billion, AMD about $2.5 billion, Broadcom, Micron and Microsoft about $1 billion each, and TSMC about $500 million, according to the filing. Those shares will not be subject to the usual post-IPO lock-up period.

Anthropic expects net proceeds of about $84.6 billion from the IPO and the private placement combined at the midpoint, which it plans to use for general corporate purposes, including investment in compute and infrastructure, according to the filing.

The company reported revenue of $33.2 billion for the first nine months of 2026, up from $2.5 billion a year earlier. It posted a net loss of $104 billion over the period, most of it from a $94.3 billion non-cash charge tied to the rising value of its convertible notes and other instruments; its operating loss was $1.26 billion. Anthropic has also committed to about $900.9 billion of spending, mostly on compute and infrastructure, of which about $70.7 billion is due within the next 12 months.

Anthropic will list as a public benefit corporation with an unusual governance structure. Its Long-Term Benefit Trust, a body of financially disinterested trustees, will elect a majority of its board, while a single share of Class F stock held by an entity owned by its seven co-founders will carry 50.1% of the company's voting power on most matters, according to the filing. As a result, Anthropic will be a "controlled company" under Nasdaq rules, and holders of its Class A shares will have limited influence over major decisions, the filing warns.

The range comes after a turbulent week for AI stocks. Some major investors told The Wall Street Journal last week that Anthropic's expected $1.5 trillion valuation would likely need to come down following a sell-off in AI stocks, with one suggesting a figure closer to $1 trillion. Anthropic had been eyeing a valuation of around $2 trillion as recently as September, according to an earlier draft filing.

The IPO's order book is already covered at the proposed range, CNBC reported, citing people familiar with the matter.

This article was drafted with the assistance of Claude, an AI model developed by Anthropic.

Only Known Handwritten Draft of Martin Luther King Jr.’s ‘I Have a Dream’ Speech Could Top $10 M. at Christie’s

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Christie’s announced Monday that it will offer the only known handwritten manuscript of Martin Luther King Jr.‘s 1963 speech at the March on Washington for Jobs and Freedom, better known as the “I Have a Dream” speech.

The manuscript has seven yellow pages of King’s handwriting, including edits and cross-outs. It will be offered in a single-lot sale on January 22, 2027. The lot will carry an estimate on request, though Christie’s said it expects the manuscript to sell for more than $10 million. Related Articles Rare Jan van Huysum Pendant Paintings Head to Christie's for $10.7 M. A Seagram Heir, a Bankrupt Hedge Funder, and More: Consignors for the November Marquee Sales Revealed

In a statement, Peter Klarnet, a manuscripts specialist at Christie’s, called the piece “a treasure of American history.”

“When I first saw these pages, and realized what they were, I was astounded,” Klarnet said. “It gives you chills to look at it. Reading along, you’re there in the moment with Dr. King, watching him compose it and making edits in real time. It captures a moment that literally changed the course of our nation and makes it very real and very human. It’s an honor and privilege that Christie’s has an opportunity to present it to the world.”

According to Christie’s, the pages were found in a desk drawer that belonged to Edward T. Clayton, head of public relations for the Southern Christian Leadership Conference (SCLC), the civil rights organization King led until his assassination in 1968. Clayton was also a speechwriter and editor for King and helped write the speech. His wife, Xernona Clayton, a civil rights leader and philanthropist, found the manuscript in the late 1990s. She recognized the speech but didn’t know it was the only handwritten final draft, so she put it back in her late husband’s desk. In 2024, she and her adviser, Eddie Bridgeman, brought the manuscript to Christie’s.

“I’m just glad I looked in the drawer,” Xernona Clayton said in a statement. “The two of them gelled with writing and words. They would go back and forth and there was such respect. Dr. King, who was a great thinker, said Ed was so good at getting his words on paper and putting them in the right place at the right time. I enjoyed watching them. I was privileged, and now the world will have the opportunity to see those words of wisdom that continue to inspire hope, freedom, and the pursuit of equality.”

The handwritten manuscript doesn’t include the words “I have a dream,” because King famously improvised that part of the speech. It does include his famous conclusion: “Free at last, free at last; thank God almighty, we are free at last.”

Christie’s said proceeds from the sale will go to charities “furthering the legacies of Ms. Clayton and Dr. King,” including the Xernona Clayton Foundation and the King Center in Atlanta.

“We are also grateful to join with Ms. Clayton to ensure that the sale of this draft helps support the great work of The King Center and our mother, Mrs. Coretta Scott King, who would be 100 this upcoming year,” Bernice A. King, the Kings‘ daughter, said in a statement.

The manuscript will be on view at the National Center for Civil and Human Rights in Atlanta from November 16–22. It will then be shown at Christie’s New York from January 15–21.

Adobe brings AI agents and new creative tools to Photoshop, Premiere, and Firefly

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Adobe is pouring more AI into its products, including Photoshop, Premiere, Illustrator, and InDesign, in a major product feature announcement at its MAX event in Miami. The company is also expanding AI capabilities in its creative tool, Firefly, and releasing a Model Context Protocol (MCP) that lets other chatbots like ChatGPT, Claude, or Gemini use Adobe features.

Adobe’s Firefly app is moving into Figma’s territory with a new prototyping tool in private beta called Builder, which lets users create designs to show off ideas. There is also a new tool to create on-brand graphs, along with an image editing tool that lets you change part of an image without changing other objects.

A new AI-powered video tool in Firefly will allow users to assemble a video from clips and other elements like images and sound effects. The company is making its agent proactive and always on so that it can get more context about work and be present for any action. Adobe is also making it easier for people to take quick action, like removing the background or generating a thumbnail for a video.

The company has already added a ton of AI features across its apps like Photoshop, Express, Premiere, and Illustrator. At the MAX event, Adobe is continuing the trend. Photoshop is getting a new relighting feature and sliders for prompt-based generative styles; Premiere will have a new color grading system for professionals and an object removal feature for video; and Lightroom has a way to correct blinking eyes and a blemish removal tool.

Adobe already offers a lot of third-party models from companies like Google, Runway, ElevenLabs, Black Forest Labs, OpenAI, Kling, and ByteDance. One of its newer acquisitions, Topaz Labs, is releasing two image models, called Wonder 4 for upscaling and denoising, and ImageHDR, to turn standard images into HDR.

The company already has ChatGPT and Claude integrations to let users access some of its product functions. Now, Adobe is releasing a local MCP to expose more features of its Creative Cloud apps to users.

Adobe vice president Deepa Subramaniam told TechCrunch that the company is still figuring out what capabilities to expose and will rely on user feedback.

“We are still figuring out where and when and how to draw the line. What we’re putting out there is a number of endpoints and capabilities across Illustrator, Photoshop, After Effects, and Premiere. We’re just going to see how people use it, what they’re trying to do, and keep showing more. We don’t have any need or desire to sort of limit what the MCP endpoint can expose. What we really want to do is to support that creative workflow, how you choose to do it,” said Subramaniam.

Last month, Adobe rival Figma co-founder Dylan Field said at an event in India that MCP is not a perfect standard and benefits frontier labs more.

Grand Theft Auto VI pre-load now available, PS5 Pro support confirmed

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Rockstar Games has released a new trailer for Grand Theft Auto VI to commemorate the start of pre-loading ahead of the game’s November 19 release, as well as confirmed that the game will support PlayStation Spectral Super Resolution and ray-traced shadows on PlayStation 5 Pro.

Grand Theft Auto VI has been fully developed from the ground up to provide the best possible experience for the current generation of consoles, targeting 30 frames per second while pushing both platforms to their absolute limits to bring the vast and rich world of Leonida to life—while maintaining the density and visual fidelity of all its environments and inhabitants.

The PlayStation 5 Pro offers breathtaking visuals, with improved clarity through PlayStation Spectral Super Resolution (PSSR) and ray-traced shadows only available on PlayStation 5 Pro.

Grand Theft Auto VI for PlayStation 5 includes high-fidelity haptic feedback fully integrated across the game on DualSense controllers and adjustable via accessibility settings, improved spatial audio rendering so all in-game sounds take full advantage of Tempest 3D Audio, and improved load times, making Grand Theft Auto VI play best on PlayStation 5 consoles.

The game will also be available via Xbox Cloud Gaming on Xbox Series X|S and Xbox One consoles, utilizing Xbox Series S graphics settings. And as with all cloud streaming, connection speeds and other technical factors may influence game performance in ways that would not impact local gameplay.

Grand Theft Auto VI will also fully support Remote Play on PlayStation and Xbox.

Grand Theft Auto VI is due out for PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series on November 19.

Michael Carrick's future as Manchester United head coach is little closer to being resolved.

Despite widespread reports on Monday night claiming a decision has been made to sack the under-pressure manager, Carrick arrived at the club’s Carrington training ground on Tuesday and oversaw training with a depleted group due to international commitments.

Live blog: All the latest Man Utd news with Carrick on the brink

It was expected that Carrick would be sacked on Tuesday, with an official announcement to come soon after, but BBC Sport report that it will be at least another day until their is concrete clarity.

Carrick’s future is set to be discussed at a meeting involving Sir Jim Ratcliffe and senior club executives in Monaco on Wednesday.

Mauricio Pochettino is considered the frontrunner for the job should Carrick depart. Eddie Howe, Julian Nagelsmann and Antonio Conte are all thought to be interested in the role.

Persona 4 Revival's Chie Has Thighs That Were "Quite Challenging" To Design

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

In 2024, Atlus released Persona 3 Reload, giving the game far more than a fresh coat of paint. New character models, fully voiced Social Links, and a whole new battle system headlined a huge roster of changes. The same looks set to be said for Persona 4 Revival, which is due for release early next year.

Atlus has been slowly breaking down the changes with news drops and character introduction videos, and the game's development team occasionally speaks to the press about what makes 4 Revival special.

A new interview has just surfaced with Persona general producer Kazuhisa Wada, Persona 4 Revival's director Daisuke Yajima, and sound composer Ryota Kozuka. The trio discussed the challenges they've faced during the development of the upcoming reimagining, and surprisingly, one of them was the design of Chie Satonaka's thighs.

Chie Satonaka is one of Persona 4's main party members. She goes to the same high school as the game's protagonist and is also a lover of Kung-Fu. This juxtaposition of character proved difficult for the team at Atlus.

In an interview published on the Famitsu website today, director Yamija expressed his desire to "maintain the same level of quality" throughout Persona 4 Revival as was found in Persona 3 Reload. Despite being full of enthusiasm, Yajima revealed that this was "quite challenging," especially as the team had to "adjust the CG models and illustrations" after it had begun creating cutscenes.

When asked for specifics, Yajima said, "There was a discussion about Chie's thighs. The CG model was made with a 'thickness' that would make kung fu-like motions look good, but the illustration was drawn with a 'thinness' that was faithful to the original character design, so there was a slight gap between the two."

It was a debate that, according to Yajima, continued for as long as the game was in production.

"We eventually managed to find a compromise, but even with the bust-up illustration, each staff member's attention to detail never ceased, with comments like, 'She looks a little too young, so I want to redraw her to look more like a high school student,' or 'Wouldn't it be more 'like 4' if we deliberately made the coloring more anime-like?' The event cutscenes were polished relentlessly right up until the end of development."

Meta specifically says this issue does not affect Ray-Ban or Oakley-branded Meta glasses.

I can’t recall ever seeing an email from Meta with the subject “Action required” before, which tells me this is potentially a big deal. When asked, a Meta spokesperson told me that there was no additional info beyond what’s presented in the email, which you can see below:

The new software update brings your glasses to version 130, which includes a special software hook to keep them from getting too warm. While I haven’t seen any specific reports of these glasses models overheating, there are plenty of Reddit threads citing overheating issues on Ray-Ban Meta glasses over the years. Ironically, the one post I found related to sweat-related overheating was from a year ago on Ray-Ban Meta glasses, which this software update does not address.

Club Kid's My Favorite Movie This Year (So Far), And I'm Obsessed With Its Take On Gay Nightlife

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

15+ Sexually Explicit Movies To Watch On Hulu

I Saw Wicker. It Was So Worth Seeing In A Theater (Packed Full Of Women)

New And Recent Movies Streaming: Big Films We Can Finally Watch At Home - Updated November 2026

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

It's one of the most authentically queer movies I've ever seen.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

2026 has been a solid year for movies, with smaller-budget movies like Obsession managing to make it big at the box office. There were also a number of daring new A24 movies, and that studio is offering another daring entry with Jordan Firstman's Club Kid. I was able to catch an early screening of the dramedy, which had me laughing and crying in equal measures throughout its brisk 121-minute runtime. Club Kid is my favorite movie of the entire year (so far), and I'm truly obsessed with its portrayal of gay nightlife.

Over the past few years, there's been a ton of conversation about the importance of LGBTQ+ representation in the media. While we got a steamy book-to-screen adaptation with Heated Rivalry, Club Kid offers an incredibly authentic look at nightlife in the community, warts and all. As a gay man in my mid-30s, I thought it was impressive how the depictions were, allowing me to relate intimately with this acclaimed new film.

Jordan Firstman is the writer, director and star of Club Kid, and deserves all the flowers he's received at festivals like Cannes and TIFF. While audiences might know him from his scene-stealing roles in English Teacher (streaming with a Hulu subscription) and Ms. Marvel, he takes center stage as protagonist Peter. He's a party promoter and habitual drug user who has been raging in the New York City nightlife scene for a decade, and is feeling the effects that his nocturnal proclivities take on him.

When Peter is fired from his job and arguably at his lowest, he's surprised to learn he has a son named Arlo (Reggie Absolom), the result of having sex with a woman in a darkroom years ago. What follows is a grittier take on Big Daddy, and we watch Peter and his son bond when he's suddenly dropped off at his door following the death of Arlo's mother.

Throughout the movie, we're shown an unapologetic and unfiltered look at the gay nightlife scene, including the drug use that can often accompany it. The movie's opening sequence shows Peter at the height of his career as a promoter. I was immediately struck by how real the club scene was, showing the various mini-conversations that happen in a crowded bar. Peter and his friends also engage in a mixture of drugs, especially GHB, ketamine and cocaine. It's when he's crossfaded that he eventually has sex with a woman, in a scene set in the venue's darkroom, where folks go to engage in anonymous sex.

Darkrooms have a long history in the LGBTQ+ community, and have been portrayed onscreen in projects like Harvey Fierstein's Torch Song Trilogy. While some audiences might balk at the scene in Club Kid, I have to applaud Firstman for showing them in such a real way, potentially educating straight moviegoers in the process.

Peter's relationship with drugs is also a major part of this movie, and I once again was shocked by how close to home it felt. I've seen firsthand how alluring and insidious drug use can be in nightlife, and have lost friends to it. Firstman's protagonist is given reason to change his lifestyle when he suddenly becomes a parent, but there are so many queer folks who are never able to separate themselves from the scene. While I've never had the experience of meeting a child I didn't know I had, I couldn't help but relate to the story told throughout the film.

Club Kid gets its wide theatrical release November 6th as part of the 2026 movie release list. We'll have to see if another movie manages to beat it as my favorite movie of the year... but I doubt it. Now let Jordan Firstman direct whatever else he wants.

After working in administrative theater for a year in New York, he started as the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. He's since been able to work himself up to reviews, phoners, and press junkets-- and is now able to appear on camera with some of his favorite actors... just not as he would have predicted as a kid. He's particularly proud of covering horror franchises like Scream and Halloween, as well as movie musicals like West Side Story. Vikings' 17:28 time of possession was their lowest in franchise history

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The Minnesota Vikings have played 1,064 games in the history of their franchise, which started in 1961. Before yesterday, they had never played a game in which they possessed the ball for less than 17 and a half minutes. Now they have.

The Vikings’ time of possession of 17:28 in Monday night’s 23-6 loss to the Bills was their lowest in franchise history.

The previous franchise low was 17:38, set in the NFC Championship Game after the 2000 season, when the Vikings lost to the Giants 41-0.

On Monday night, the Vikings’ offense could not stay on the field. The first quarter set the tone: They had three possessions, which were two three-and-outs and one possession on which they lost a fumble on the first play. Their longest possession of the night lasted 3:11, and on that possession they ran five plays, totaled just 10 yards, and punted.

The Vikings’ defense played well, but not so well that the Bills weren’t able to sustain some long drives, including three drives that took more than six minutes off the clock. The Bills gained 22 first downs; the Vikings gained just nine.

The Bills’ 42:32 time of possession was their second-best in franchise history, trailing only a 1989 game in which they possessed the ball for 42:53 and beat the Jets 34-3.

Herbert Direct are a family-run business based in Broadbridge Heath, supplying kitchen and furniture components to customers across the UK. Founded by John and Sharon Herbert in 2003, the company has strong local roots.

Their services span aerospace logistics, global logistics and fulfilment, supporting customers across the UK and internationally.

5 Costco Baked Goods To Buy And 4 To Skip

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

The holidays are just around the corner. Between Thanksgiving and Christmas, you'll have Friendsgivings, work parties, school functions, and every other conceivable event that may require you to bring a baked good. Sometimes, it's a bit too much, which is why there's absolutely no shame in going the store-bought route instead of firing up the oven at home.

Prices are as of the date of publication and may vary based on region.

Costco is a godsend if you're hosting a large group for brunch. There are a handful of breakfast-centric items on this list, but first and foremost are the bagels. I snagged a bag of the plain bagels, but Costco has several other flavors, including Parmesan Cheese and the new Pretzel Bagels.

An 8-pack of Plain Bagels retails for $5.66, an unsurprisingly great value given the store. True to form, the bagels are also quite large, making them worthy of a full meal on their own. While they don't come pre-sliced, I prefer to buy bagels whole as it helps keep them fresh. Each bagel is soft, chewy, and toasts up perfectly.

If bagels are the main focus of your meal, there's no need to overthink things. Costco bagels are great, even if they don't come from a shop focused entirely on them. This is the perfect, no-stress addition to any breakfast and a definite freezer staple.

Because our relationship is built on trust, I'm going to come right out and say the "skips" on this list aren't hard passes, but more likely items you can survive without. Frankly, Costco doesn't make many bad products, and the Buttermilk Biscuits are one such example.

These things are huge. I honestly don't think I've ever seen a biscuit this large, let alone ten of them for $7.93. I'll jump into the review in a moment, but the reason the biscuits landed in my skip pile is that they're one of the few baked goods I purchased that came with cooking instructions and the only one that I think really needs to be cooked to be properly enjoyed.

A cold biscuit is, in my opinion, a sad biscuit, and while Costco's Buttermilk Biscuits are serviceable at room temperature, they're worlds better when warmed up. You can toss them in the oven or do what I did and zap each one in the microwave for about 30 seconds. The result is a steaming, fluffy, buttery biscuit roughly the size of a baseball. These are really good biscuits, but if they need to be warm to really enjoy them, there are more convenient options in the Costco bakery.

France has delivered a lot of wonderful things to the United States — looking at you, Lady Liberty — but the best cultural exchange may have been the croissant. Almost impossibly airy, flaky, and absurdly buttery, the croissant is a hugely versatile pastry. It can go sweet, it can go savory, or you can just munch on one as a snack.

A 12-pack of Costco Croissants is just $6.80. For comparison, my local Target has an 8-pack of Pillsbury Original Crescent Rolls for $3.39. Don't get me wrong, I love those fresh-baked little bundles of joy, but a single Costco croissant is equivalent to multiple of the smaller home-baked ones. The value is unbeatable, and the flavor is close behind. These croissants are everything you could hope for, and, thanks to their size, you can really get creative with them. Slice one in half and make a sandwich or, as I did, take 8 and put them into a fantastic French toast bake. It's culinary cultural inception and a great way to use a very tasty baked good.

Costco's muffins have a long and surprisingly winding history filled with recipe and size changes that bordered on national scandals. Thankfully, it seems that the muffins have once again found their sweet spot as some of the better options from the Costco bakery. Of course, this is a skip, though, so while I enjoy the Vanilla Chocolate Chip Muffins (which retail for $7.93 for an 8-count package), you can do better.

Costco has a number of different muffin flavors. Blueberry, Vanilla Chocolate Chip, and Double Chocolate are the standard options, but seasonal and rotating offerings include everything from Blueberry Creme to Pumpkin Streusel. I've reviewed Costco's Double Chocolate Muffins before and they're fantastic; rich, filled with chocolate, and riding that fine line between a breakfast pastry and a dessert.

The Vanilla Chocolate Chip Muffins are plenty tasty in their own right. Each one is remarkably soft and spongy, with a sweetness that makes it taste like a supersized Little Debbie treat. The chocolate chips are large, but not super evenly distributed. These are perfectly serviceable muffins you're almost guaranteed to enjoy, but Costco has better muffins, so these are an easy skip.

Let's start with the fact that this pack has not one, but two coconut-based bars. The Wildberry Macaroons are admittedly tasty thanks to a tart berry jam that rests between a bevy of toasted coconut flakes and a soft, buttery macaroon base. Yes, that's double coconut in one bar, but a single, good coconut option is just fine by me. The issue is that the Macaroon Madness Bars are a second coconut-based treat disguised as a blondie. Until I took a bite, I had no idea that this bar was also filled with coconut. It's not as good or as interesting as the Wildberry Macaroon, so this feels like a total waste of space.

The Artisan Rolls are sold in a 12-pack for $6.80. They're precut and, you guessed it, they're big. The ciabatta-style bread is chewy, airy, and perfect for sandwiches. Especially leftover sandwiches. Perhaps after a food-centric holiday. What I'm getting at is that you need to buy these for the day after Thanksgiving to make some spectacular, carbtastic, leftover sandwiches. They're hearty enough to handle whatever combination of turkey, gravy, potatoes, stuffing, and cranberry sauce you can throw at them, but not so dense that you'll fill up on bread. A good roll in the right setting can make a meal, and Costco's Artisan Rolls are perfect for making the kind of sandwich that inevitably leads to a wonderful nap.

I've waxed poetic about Costco's portion sizes thus far because, for the most part, I can find a way to normalize a massive amount of baked goods in the right setting. There is an exception to every rule, though, and the baguette is mine.

Priced at $5.66 for a two-pack of baguettes, this is an almost absurd amount of bread. Each baguette is nearly two feet long. Combined, that's the height of a 6-year-old. Unlike some of the other items I tried, the baguette also seems to have the shortest shelf life. You either need to eat it that day or freeze it immediately to avoid the outer crust from becoming chewy. If you're hosting a spaghetti dinner that night, this is a decent enough option, but it's still a bread that simply doesn't stay good for long.

I'm not entirely out on the Costco Baguette, but beyond being generally impractical, it's not a product that blew me away with either taste or texture. It's fine, and with so many other things to choose from, fine isn't enough.

In the world of sweet breakfast pastries, I think the Danish is too often forgotten. It's basically the flaky, buttery goodness of a croissant paired with pie filling. Honestly, how could that combo ever be bad?

Costco's danishes come in packs of four. There are a few unique flavors to choose from, with Cheese, Cherry, and Almond as the standards, while seasonal favorites include Strawberry Cream Cheese and Apple Cinnamon. The mix-and-match portion of the name comes from the pricing: You can get two four-packs for $13.61, which is what I did to end up with a Cheese and an Apple.

True to form, these Danishes are hefty and really tasty. The fillings are sweet, but not overwhelmingly so, and the pastry portion is baked to perfection. The Cheese flavor is especially great for anyone who lives on the more savory side of the spectrum, but still likes to dabble in the occasional sweet treat. The Apple was my favorite, though. I like any excuse to eat a classic apple pie for breakfast, and Costco's Danish is the perfect one.

I purchased all nine Costco baked goods at the same time and brought them home to photograph and sample. My evaluations were based on flavor, texture, and overall value.

BT won't launch Starlink deal until people forget about Ukraine

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

CEO Alison Kirkby is waiting for the ‘noise to die down.’

The UK's largest telecoms company has paused the launch of its Starlink tie-up, but not for the reason you may expect. Speaking to the Financial Times, BT CEO Alison Kirkby said the launch would wait until "the noise has died down" due to Elon Musk's personal intervention in Ukraine. Kirkby said that BT was "cautious on how Starlink might be viewed at the moment considering what happened in Ukraine a couple of weeks ago."

Starlink, the satellite internet arm of Elon Musk's SpaceX, has provided infrastructure support to Ukraine while it defends itself against the Russian invasion. In October, Musk reportedly ordered Starlink to slow down the service used by the Ukrainian military, sparking international outrage. The move caused alarm both in demonstrating how much power Musk holds, and raised questions about his reliability as a business partner.

Despite this, BT is undeterred that the deal will launch, saying that it will offer a "giant leap" for rural connectivity in the UK. The Financial Times also quoted Kirkby in a statement, which added that "offering the best products for our customers means working with the best partners" and that Starlink would launch "in the coming months." Who knew that "the best partners" also includes someone with a proven track record of messing with service quality on the basis of nothing but his own whim?

Anthropic sets IPO price range of $70 to $78 per share to reach an implied valuation of $1.404 trillion

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Anthropic intends to push ahead with its IPO despite shakier markets and the broader pullback from AI investing.

The Claude-maker said in an updated IPO filing on Tuesday that it would be offering a total of 825 million Class A shares at an estimated price range of $70 to $78 per share. The sale is expected to raise anywhere from $57.75 billion to $64.35 billion.

The company could end up being worth a hefty $1.404 trillion if its shares list at the top of the price range, on a fully diluted basis. That would represent a significant jump up from the $965 billion valuation Anthropic reached in a May fundraise, but it’s still lower than the $2 trillion the company was reportedly targeting a couple months ago.

Separately, Anthropic said it had struck a deal to privately sell $26 billion of non-voting Class N stock at the IPO price to AMD, Broadcom, Google, Micron, Microsoft, Nvidia and TSMC. Google and Nvidia are investing $10 billion apiece.

All up, Anthropic estimates net proceeds to reach somewhere between $84.6 billion and $93.7 billion (the latter if the IPO underwriters exercise their option to buy additional shares).

Underwriters, including Goldman Sachs and Morgan Stanley, will have the option to buy up to 123.75 million more shares from Anthropic at the IPO price.

As a public benefit corporation, the founders hold a Class F share with 50.1% of the voting power. The Long-Term Benefit Trust will also elect a board majority, so Class A buyers will have limited influence.

Anthropic intends to start trading on the Nasdaq close to November 19, per reporting from CNBC, under the ticker symbol “ANT”. The company expects up to 20% of its IPO shares to be allocated to retail investors.

Anthropic also gave out a few new details in the updated filing. The company said its run-rate revenue reached $81 billion by the end of October, about $10 billion more than at the end of September.

The company’s third-quarter revenue came in at $17 billion, and nine-month revenue was $33.2 billion. The company reported a nine-month net loss of $104 billion, with compute and infrastructure commitments hitting $900.9 billion. It has nearly $132 billion in cash and short-term investments.

OpenAI, Anthropic’s biggest rival, was reportedly valued at $1.25 trillion last week after it raised $11 billion, nearly half the $20 billion it had first tried to raise.

Buyers Dig Beta Cinema’s Norwegian Oscar Entry ‘Growth Of The Soil’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Beta Cinema has unveiled a raft of international deals for Hans Petter Moland’s Norwegian Oscar entry Growth of the Soil.

The film has sold to North America (Greenwich Entertainment), Germany & Austria (Neue Visionen), Italy (Teodora Film), Spain (A Contracorriente Films), UK & Ireland (Conic), Benelux (Cherry Pickers), Switzerland (Frenetic Films), Greece (Weird Wave), the Baltics (Scanorama), Hungary (Vertigo Media), Bulgaria (Beta Film), India (Big Tree Entertainment), Indonesia (Falcon Pictures), Brazil (Imovision) and Australia and New Zealand (Palace Films). Related Stories Acquisitions Claes Bang Heinrich Himmler Masseur Drama 'I Is Another' Sells Wide For Beta Cinema - AFM News Jeff Daniels Leading Comedy 'The Reception' About Chaotic Scottish Wedding; Trudie Styler Directing & Beta Launching TIFF Sales

The feature has also enjoyed success closer to home, where the Scandinavian release is handled by Scanbox, with the epic love story taking the top slot at the box office in Norway in its opening weekend. Watch on Deadline

Based on Nobel Prize Winning novel, Growth of the Soil tells the story of Isak, a man without a past who crosses the mountains in search of somewhere to build a life.

High above the tree line, he finds an unforgiving stretch of land and, eventually, Inger, a woman whose resilience matches his own. Together they create a home and family from almost nothing, until time, ambition and a changing world begin to transform both their relationship and the landscape around them.

Herbert Nordrum (The Worst Person in the World) and Asta Kamma August (The Hypnosis) costar, alongside to Gard B. Eidsvold (Troll), Anders Baasmo (Kon-Tiki), Oddgeir Thune (Loveable) and Sjur Vatne Brean (Out Stealing Horses).

Moland, whose credits include Out Stealing Horses, In Order Of Disappearance, A Somewhat Gentle Man and Zero Kelvin, co-wrote the screenplay with actor and writer Gard B. Eidsvold.

The film was shot by Oskar Dahlsbakken, whose credits include Possession, Munch and The Arctic Convoy, with an original score by Danish composer Kaspar Kaae (Fjord, In Order Of Disappearance). It is produced by Håkon Øverås for 4 ½ Fiction, in co-production with Scanbox.

Growth of the Soil world premiered as a Special Presentation at Toronto, and then made its European debut at San Sebastián International Film Festival, where it won the Silver Shell for Best Leading Performance and the Jury Prize for Best Cinematography.

It has since played at the Warsaw, the Rio, Denver, Mar del Plata and opened the Bergen International Film Festival. It also is shortlisted for this year’s European Film Awards for the Best Feature Film category.

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Moody’s Taps Chainlink to Send Credit Ratings Across Multiple Blockchains

Chainlink and Moody’s Ratings have completed a pilot showing how credit ratings could be beamed across multiple blockchains at once, another sign that the traditional machinery of finance is steadily moving onchain.

Announced Tuesday, the test used Chainlink’s Cross-Chain Interoperability Protocol, or CCIP, to distribute Moody’s ratings simultaneously across both permissioned and permissionless blockchain networks.

It ran through the Moody’s Ratings Token Integration Engine, or TIE, the system the 125-year-old ratings giant built to disseminate credit assessments and related data within blockchain environments. The pilot, the companies said, preserved Moody’s existing publication and governance processes while proving the ratings could travel across chains through a single interoperable layer.

The logic is straightforward: as more bonds, funds, and other assets are issued as blockchain tokens, the investors trading them will expect the credit intelligence they rely on to be available wherever those assets live, rather than stopping at any one network’s edge.

Moody’s brings real experience here, having rated or assessed more than 70 digital issuances, including digital bonds and tokenized funds, between January 2017 and October 2026.

“As more financial assets move onchain, credit intelligence has to move with them, across chains and through every rating change,” said Johann Eid, chief business officer at Chainlink Labs, calling the pilot a foundational piece of what institutional capital needs to adopt tokenization at scale.

Fabian Astic, Moody’s global head of digital economy, argued that “the future of tokenized finance is unlikely to reside on a single blockchain, platform, or market infrastructure,” and that investors will increasingly expect ratings regardless of the underlying technology.

The effort builds on Moody’s steady push onchain. Earlier this year, the firm launched TIE on the institutional Canton Network before extending it to Solana, making its ratings machine-readable on a major public blockchain.

Chainlink, meanwhile, has positioned CCIP as the connective tissue for institutional tokenization, recently rolling out an upgraded version and reporting tens of billions of dollars in cross-chain value secured as banks and asset managers experiment with moving assets between networks.

The pilot, though, ran entirely on a testnet using representative test data, with no actual rating actions published onchain and no live dissemination involved.

It demonstrated technical capability rather than a launched product, but the companies said the model is designed to accommodate future rating updates under appropriate governance. For now, it’s a proof of concept, albeit one pointing toward a future where even credit ratings are just another piece of data flowing across blockchains.

Adobe’s Creative Agent Wants To Do the Photo and Video Editing Tasks You Don’t Want To

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Adobe’s Creative Agent Wants To Do the Photo and Video Editing Tasks You Don’t Want To

Adobe has been developing agentic AI for years, and at this year’s Adobe MAX in Miami, it looks like it has finally been fully unleashed. Adobe is ushering in significant expansions to the Adobe Creative Agent, which users can direct using text across not only Adobe’s Firefly AI platform but also Creative Cloud apps like Photoshop and Premiere.

The Adobe Creative Agent is designed to help users with complex, multi-step tasks across Adobe’s creative software. This agent works directly alongside Adobe’s expanded AI Assistants (beta) across its applications, including Photoshop and Premiere.

The overarching goal, as Adobe has discussed before, is to use AI for repetitive, tedious, or otherwise less creative tasks. For example, organizing files, generating masks, and replacing one color with another are all relatively uncreative tasks. The ideas behind these tasks, like why the user wants a specific object in their photo selected and what local edits to do it, are the artistic ones.

“Since its introduction earlier this year, creators have used it for everything from batch retouching portraits to converting product shots into short social videos. Creators remain in the director’s chair, guiding the work and making creative decisions as their projects evolve,” Adobe says.

Of course, Adobe has noted before and continues to say that it wants to create AI agent tools to empower its users, and then those users can decide how and when they want to deploy them.

“We’re powering a new era of creativity where creators and creative professionals at every experience level have the tools, models and agents they need to bring their biggest ideas to life and stand out with work that reflects their unique taste, style and vision, while staying in control of every outcome,” says Anil Chakravarthy, President and incoming Chief Executive Officer at Adobe.

“With demand for content growing across every format and channel, we’re delivering new innovations across our product portfolio that expand how creative work happens and put the power of Adobe’s tools within reach for even more creatives,” Chakravarthy continues.

Adobe’s new Firefly-powered agents can be always on, tasked with doing certain things as needed. For example, if someone needs to turn an image into a deliverable across different formats, the Adobe Creative Agent can do that thanks to new Photoshop tools like Generative Recompose.

The Adobe Creative Agent also works with other new AI-powered tools inside Photoshop and Premiere, like Relight and Generative Restyle in Photoshop and Generative Remove (beta) in Premiere.

Not every Adobe user necessarily wants to use Adobe’s own agent. They may already be using one in other applications. Today at Adobe MAX, Adobe also previewed local MCP (Model Context Protocol) integration, which will let creative pros direct their preferred third-party agent to make changes inside Photoshop, Premiere, and other Creative Cloud apps.

Adobe’s new AI Assistants, powered by the Adobe Creative Agent, are now available in beta in Adobe’s flagship desktop apps, except Photoshop, where the AI Assistant is available only in Photoshop (beta). Adobe’s local MCP integrations will be available in private beta early next year.

KORA builds AI models and agents into a new Linux OS bound for Qualcomm Snapdragon PCs

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Desktop software company KORA Systems Inc. today unveiled KORA, a Linux-based operating system that builds artificial intelligence models and agents into the system itself.

Qualcomm Technologies Inc. has spent close to a year working with the company to bring it to Snapdragon-powered PCs.

The launch goes after what KORA sees as a ceiling on AI in current desktop computers. Most AI software on macOS and Windows runs inside a browser tab or an app sandbox, according to the company, with little view of the computer underneath.

Because both platforms are closed, KORA argues, the integration stops at that point. Linux, by KORA’s account, is the only operating system open enough to be rebuilt around models and agents from the kernel up.

The core of that rebuild is an inference engine KORA calls Cortex. It pools the central processor, graphics processor and neural processing unit in a PC, plus any other machines on the network, and treats them as one computer. Models run locally on the user’s hardware by default, and frontier models get pulled in for heavier jobs.

On screen, the usual layout of folders and apps is gone. What KORA calls neural workspaces take its place, each one arranged around the task a user is working on. The user sets the permissions for any applications and agents sandboxed inside a workspace. Under all of it sits a neural file system, and the company says memory and context stay on the local machine.

Outside developers can build on the same pieces KORA’s own applications use through its software development kit, which opens up primitives such as memory, semantic search and email. Any model, AI service or agent will be able to integrate at the system level, the company said. An agentic service could even run as part of the operating system and take its identity, memory and compute from KORA.

Chief Executive Kirt McMaster called the desktop operating system “the ultimate harness.” About 45 million people account for 80% of spending on AI, he said, and almost all of their work happens on desktops running operating systems built 40 years ago. By 2033 the group will top 150 million people spending more than $400 billion a year, according to KORA’s own estimates. “Nobody has built an operating system for them,” McMaster said.

KORA grew out of Playtron Corp., a company McMaster founded to build a Linux alternative to Windows for gaming PCs. Playtron’s engineers wrote GameOS as a complete Linux operating system and shipped it on gaming devices, and the same team has been building KORA on that foundation since November 2025. GameOS carries on inside the new system as GameFrame, a one-click play mode that turns the desktop into a console for PC games.

With Cyanogen, Android and GameOS behind them, the founders count KORA as their fourth operating system. Chief Business Officer John Lagerling led Android business development at Google LLC, and he said he joined when the platform had 100,000 users and stayed until it had a billion. Openness got it there, in his view, and AI needs the same treatment.

The company believes “50 superpowered engineers can take on Windows,” according to Chief Technology Officer Franck De Girolami. His engineers now work side by side with AI models and agents, he said, to the point that “KORA is built inside KORA.”

The Qualcomm work is laid out in a separate joint release today. The result so far is a complete Linux desktop running on PCs with Snapdragon X2 Elite chips, the two companies said, and Cortex now spreads AI workloads across the whole chip. Qualcomm engineers worked on the project throughout, and the chipmaker is also pushing X2 Elite support into the mainline Linux kernel.

Those machines can run a 26 billion-parameter language model locally, according to the release. Kedar Kondap, Qualcomm’s senior vice president and general manager of compute and gaming, said the X2 Elite was designed to run advanced AI on the device. KORA has also built translation technology that lets selected Windows x86-64 applications run inside the Linux environment, so people switching over can keep using software they rely on.

The first machines to ship will run HUMAIN OS, an enterprise operating system KORA is co-developing with Saudi Arabian AI company HUMAIN. Those PCs are due in 2027, and the companies bill them as the first from hardware makers to pair a Linux-based desktop operating system with Snapdragon X2 Elite. KORA itself follows later in 2027, with pricing to be shared closer to availability.

KORA is a venture capital-backed startup that counts Samsung Next, Square Enix Holdings Co. Ltd., Polychain Capital LP, Van Eck Associates Corp., Circle Internet Group Inc. and Alumni Ventures among its investors. The release did not say how much the company has raised.

Coinbase Options Flag XRP as Top Volatility Bet With 9.7% Swing Signal: What’s Next?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cryptocurrency options markets are pricing in potentially significant price swings across major digital assets through Nov. 15, with XRP recording the largest implied move compared with Bitcoin, Ethereum and Solana.

In an X post, the Coinbase Markets X account shared 7-day implied moves for top cryptocurrencies. "Crypto options imply these 1σ moves through Nov 15," the Coinbase Markets X account wrote.

Crypto options imply these 1σ moves through Nov 15: $XRP ±9.7% $SOL ±9.3% $ETH ±8.5% $BTC ±6.0% $XRP carries the widest premium to its median 7d move (1.97×). Which market delivers? https://t.co/pGXf0eFh8K

According to the latest options data, XRP carries an implied one-standard-deviation (1σ) move of ±9.7%, slightly ahead of Solana at ±9.3%. Ethereum follows with an implied move of ±8.5%, while Bitcoin has the lowest expected range at ±6%.

The figures suggest that options traders are betting on a rise in potential volatility in XRP over the coming days. However, they don't indicate whether prices will rise or fall. They may, however, provide an indication of how much price movement is currently priced into options.

XRP is notable for another reason. "XRP carries the widest premium to its median 7d move (1.97×)," Coinbase wrote. This implies options markets are pricing in a much larger move than its average weekly move.

This premium makes XRP particularly interesting as traders assess whether the token can deliver the volatility currently reflected in derivatives pricing.

At the time of writing, XRP was down 2.59% in the last 24 hours to $1.06 amid a broader market selloff.

The consumer price index data for October came in hotter than expected. The CPI for October rose 0.4%, more than the 0.3% expected from economists polled by Dow Jones and down from the 0.6% growth in September.

A December rate hike now seems likely, with Fed Governor Chris Waller suggesting even a 50-basis-point move could be appropriate.

A 9.7% increase for XRP would potentially target $1.16, whereas a 9.7% decrease from current levels would see XRP drop to $0.95. In the coming days, the markets will closely watch which direction XRP will trend next, upward or downward.

Top 10 Resorts in the East for Grooming (2027)

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Powder days are rarer out East compared to the West, so grooming crews have learned to make the most of what they have. Readers noticed: this year’s survey brought more praise than ever for the overnight teams who leave the slopes in perfect corduroy by morning.

Grooming can be a thankless job, because most skiers will never encounter a snowcat during the day. Night crews work tirelessly to ensure that skiers have the best possible time on the slopes—and our seasons would be far shorter without them.

The resorts below are where that work shines, with reliable, well-kept grooming and a mix of natural and manmade snow made for carving. Whether you’re still learning to link turns or live for fresh corduroy, read on.

Adobe puts its Coworker AI agent in GenStudio, free for existing customers

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Briefs, audience checks and on-brand asset versions now start with a chat. When Brand Intelligence and a human reviewer disagree, the human makes the call, Adobe told TNW.

Adobe’s AI agent for marketing teams, CX Enterprise Coworker, is now live inside GenStudio. That is Adobe’s software for producing and delivering marketing content. Teams ask it for what they need in plain language. The agent then carries the work across Adobe’s apps and the outside tools a team already uses.

Adobe announced the launch today at its MAX conference in Miami Beach. Coworker is free for existing licensed GenStudio customers, Adobe told TNW. All the new tools are available to customers worldwide.

It works across Adobe Experience Manager, Workfront, Brand Intelligence, Frame.io and GenStudio for Performance Marketing, depending on a customer’s licences. Teams can also use it as a standalone app, or inside Experience Manager, Workfront and Adobe Content Analytics.

Adobe says Coworker can draft campaign briefs, assess target audiences and produce versions of an asset for different channels and markets. It connects to other apps through the Model Context Protocol and Agent-to-Agent, two open standards for AI agents. Earlier this month, Adobe put the same agent inside ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Copilot.

Varun Parmar runs GenStudio and Firefly Enterprise at Adobe. He said GenStudio has delivered more than 7.8 trillion assets so far this year. Adobe told TNW the figure counts every delivery of an image, video or 3D file to a website, app or other channel through Adobe Experience Manager. One asset can count many times. GenStudio manages more than 650 million assets for its customers.

Coworker draws on Adobe Brand Intelligence, an AI that learns a brand’s standards. It learns from written guidelines and from the unwritten knowledge built up inside a company. Teams can run brand checks straight from a Coworker conversation.

TNW asked Adobe what happens when the AI and a human reviewer disagree about whether a piece is on brand. Parmar said people make the call.

“Human judgment has the final say. Brand Intelligence gives people and agents the brand context they need and checks work against brand standards at a scale no team could manage by hand, but people set the guardrails and make the call,” Parmar told TNW.

He said the system learns from every review.

“The system learns from both written guidelines and the feedback teams give during reviews in tools like Photoshop and Workfront, so every decision a reviewer makes helps it better reflect how that brand actually thinks,” he said.

Brand Intelligence also gets a new feature called intelligent asset adaptation. It resizes and rearranges a design for other formats, such as a website banner or a mobile ad. The new versions stay on brand. Adobe is building Brand Intelligence into Photoshop, Illustrator, InDesign and Firefly Creative Production for Enterprise.

Adobe backs the push with its own research, carried out by research firm Incisiv. It surveyed 607 marketing, creative and brand professionals at companies with $1 billion or more in annual revenue. It also polled 1,107 consumers and 304 business buyers in North America in July and August.

Staff closer to the work estimated that 27% of their company’s public-facing content is off-brand. Senior leaders put the figure at 9%. Adobe told TNW the gap shows how hard it is to keep to brand standards as the volume of content grows.

Of the professionals who approve content, 74% said they had signed off on off-brand work. Among those who had piloted automated brand-checking tools, 75% reported a mixed or negative experience.

In Workfront, Adobe’s project management software, a new Ideation Space gives teams a shared AI canvas. Coworker pulls past campaign results and product details onto it, and the team turns them into a structured brief. Adobe’s example is a retailer planning a Black Friday campaign.

Firefly Graph for enterprise lets teams build repeatable creative workflows from Adobe’s apps and outside AI models. Two new features are in beta. Capsules turn a finished workflow into a simple app that anyone can run, and Node Publishing lets teams build shareable custom workflow parts.

Firefly Graph outputs will also carry Content Credentials tied to the organisation, which record where an asset came from. Adobe’s 3D Digital Twin, which makes digital copies of products, can now place them in new 3D scenes. Teams and agents can also change labels and materials, generate video and set custom lighting.

Adobe has added AI to GenStudio before, and it sells its creative tools inside ChatGPT. Xfinity is bringing Coworker into how it runs campaigns.

“Bringing Adobe Coworker into how we run campaigns gives us one place to direct the work,” said Christopher Grove, vice president of GenAI and creative tech at Xfinity.

With expertise in digital marketing, product management, and branding & identity, Ana Maria Constantin develops strategies that resonate (show all) With expertise in digital marketing, product management, and branding & identity, Ana Maria Constantin develops strategies that resonate with our target audience in the software/SaaS industry. Collaboration and teamwork are paramount to her, as she loves empowering her colleagues to achieve outstanding results and unlock their full potential.

Cowbell unveils AI liability insurance for small businesses that deploy AI agents

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Cyber insurance provider Cowbell Cyber Inc. today unveiled Cowbell AI Liability Insurance, a standalone policy for U.S. small and midsized businesses that covers losses caused by the artificial intelligence agents they deploy.

The new policy is meant to deal with a problem that arises when businesses let agents move money or change customer records with nobody signing off first. Coverage hinges on a trigger the company calls an “AI Incident.” The definition takes in model drift and attacks that manipulate an agent, not just software acting without approval.

Cowbell describes an accounts payable agent misreading approval statuses and paying a supplier twice with no human check. Part of that money disappears when the supplier goes into liquidation.

Unrecovered funds like those can be covered, and the policy also pays to investigate what happened and take the agent offline. Inaccurate or unsafe output can be corrected or withdrawn at the insurer’s expense, with recipients notified.

Usage charges an agent racks up without authorization are covered too, such as when a loop goes unchecked. Cowbell also covers LLMjacking, where an outsider uses stolen credentials or application programming interface keys to run AI workloads and leaves the business holding the bill.

Claims brought by customers fall on the third-party side. Cowbell’s example there is a bookkeeping firm whose agent files client tax returns using outdated rates, and the clients sue once the penalties and interest arrive.

In a case like that, the policy can pay defense costs and any damages or settlements, and the same goes for suits alleging algorithmic discrimination or bodily injury caused by reliance on AI output. Regulatory proceedings are also covered, and where the law allows it so are certain fines and penalties.

Cowbell Chief Commercial Officer Simon Hughes said AI liability is “no longer only about people using AI,” pointing to agents that act with no human in the decision-making chain. When one goes rogue, he said, a business can take its own losses and face customer claims at the same time. Hughes said the policy was written with that double exposure in mind.

Underwriting draws partly on three AI-specific Cowbell Factors covering observability, autonomy and governance. They show underwriters how closely a business monitors its AI tools and how much authority those tools have to act on their own.

Cowbell’s Risk Advisor agent, launched in October, uses the factors to explain risk signals to policyholders. Businesses can see their own scores by connecting Gemini, Claude, ChatGPT and other tools through Cowbell Connectors.

Cowbell already sells some AI coverage. Prime One, which it launched in April for U.S. companies with $250 million to $1 billion in annual revenue, includes affirmative coverage for AI-related incidents inside a cyber policy. The new product is sold on its own and aimed at smaller businesses.

Cowbell AI Liability Insurance goes on sale Jan. 1, 2027, and will be available only through wholesale brokers on a non-admitted basis. Cowbell is a venture capital-backed company that has raised more than $200 million, including a $60 million Series C round from Zurich Insurance Group Ltd. in July 2024.

Premiere Update Brings Color Mode Out of Beta, Plus Generative Video & AI Audio Tools

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Premiere Update Brings Color Mode Out of Beta, Plus Generative Video & AI Audio Tools

Adobe is updating Premiere with a ton of AI-based tools and assistance as well as promising a new, wholly revamped approach to color grading.

The revamp to color grading has been in beta since last April but is now being rolled out to the production version of the software. Called Color Mode, it replaces the Lumetri Color Panel that has been Adobe’s approach to color grading for the last several years. This new color editor was touted as a “first-of-its-kind” color grading system and appears to be a direct response to the far more widely beloved system inside of DaVinci Resolve.

“Most Color Mode controls are bi-directional. Move your mouse up and down for one control, left and right for the other. Or press Shift for precise single-axis adjustments. Each control activates a unique heads-up display with scopes, numeric info, and real-time animated feedback that guides your creativity with confidence and clarity,” Adobe touts.

https://petapixel.com/assets/uploads/2026/11/Color-Mode.mp4

The company says that controlling this system is meant to be intuitive as “it works the way you think.” New bi-directional controls animate as they are used, and grading can be handled on single clips, groups, or entire sequences without layers.

“No more copying and pasting attributes. Actually understand how color is flowing through your timeline,” Adobe says.

Color Mode has been a point of discussion for months, but the other updates to Premiere are both newer and very much AI-based. Firstly, Adobe is deploying its Creative Agent, which is an AI assistant, into the video editor. The company says that this agent allows editors to direct and execute complex, multi-step workflows using natural language prompts. It can apply effects across every clip in a sequence, track masked subjects through a frame, and adjust color and exposure across an entire sequence simultaneously, for example.

Adobe is also adding a Generative Media Tool, which brings generative AI directly into a timeline to create missing video clips (like b-roll), sound effects, soundscapes, and background music. The AI behind it can be Adobe Firefly, but can also use the likes of Google, Kling, Runway, and Luma.

Adobe is also adding AI-powered audio tools that can clean up, separate, and refine audio. Premiere can now separate audio sources into stems that can be edited individually (a feature that was also recently added to Apple’s Logic Pro). Adobe says it is also capable of separating crosstalk, which means breaking overlapping speaking voices onto independent tracks.

Coming to the beta version, Premiere Pro is also getting a new Generative Remove tool that can remove objects from video (Adobe provides examples such as boom mics, logos, or background distractions). Editors can mask and track the object, and Premiere can fill the space with “realistic detail” while leaving the rest of the clip untouched.

Finally, as announced last week, Adobe is adding access to hundreds of thousands of licensed music tracks for use in non-commercial videos.

“Music from UMG and WMG is available starting today in Premiere on iPhone for use in personal, non-commercial videos, with availability on Android coming soon. For projects that require commercially licensed music, you can continue to access Adobe Stock Audio,” Adobe says.

Adobe is also promising that the Agentic editing experience it is bringing to Premiere Pro will also be coming to Premiere Mobile “soon.”
