Adobe expands AI assistant into creative workflows

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Adobe Inc. today announced an expansion of its CX Enterprise Coworker agentic artificial intelligence platform into its GenStudio content supply chain platform.

The company said the integration move allows creative professionals to bring AI agents into production workflows to develop, manage and deliver personalized marketing content at large scale.

The announcement, made at its Adobe MAX conference in Miami, extends CX Enterprise Coworker beyond its initial focus on customer analytics and marketing into content planning, creation and distribution. The technology provides a conversational interface for coordinating AI agents across Adobe applications and third-party systems.

Adobe said it’s aiming to reduce the manual work involved in producing marketing campaigns while maintaining human oversight and brand consistency. It’s also introducing several new capabilities across its GenStudio portfolio, including AI-assisted campaign planning, automated brand compliance, reusable creative workflows and expanded 3D product visualization.

Central to the initiative is Adobe Brand Intelligence, a machine learning system that captures an organization’s brand standards, including the unwritten practices and preferences that traditionally have resided inside employees’ heads.

“As we move to a world where creatives are directing agents, the context that’s locked in the hearts and minds of creatives is made available to agents so they can start to produce content that is on brand,” said Varun Parmar, Adobe’s senior vice president of GenStudio and Firefly for Enterprise.

Brand Intelligence will be integrated into Photoshop, Illustrator, InDesign and Firefly Creative Production for Enterprise. New intelligent asset adaptation capabilities can automatically resize and rearrange campaign designs for different media formats while preserving brand standards.

The system also incorporates continuous learning to keep guidelines current as creative practices evolve.

“It’s ingesting reviews, approvals and rejections by the creatives,” Parmar said. “Based on that information, it’s able to infer the latest guidelines.”

Administrators can inspect and override those conclusions to prevent outdated or incorrect practices from becoming embedded in automated workflows.

Another addition is Ideation Space, a collaborative AI-powered canvas within Adobe Workfront. It combines historical campaign performance, audience information and other enterprise data to help marketers develop campaign briefs.

In a demonstration, Adobe showed how Coworker could retrieve project assignments, research audience characteristics, coordinate with an AI copywriting agent and evaluate proposed designs using synthetic audience simulations. The platform can also be used to run surveys of human respondents.

Adobe is extending Firefly Graph for Enterprise with a feature called Capsules that packages complex creative workflows into simplified applications that other employees can run without mastering the underlying tools. Another feature, Node Publishing, allows teams to share reusable workflow components.

New 3D Digital Twin capabilities enable enterprises to modify product imagery, materials, lighting and camera angles and generate animations without repeatedly photographing physical products.

CX Enterprise Coworker supports external applications and custom-built systems through the Model Context Protocol, enabling enterprises to connect their existing technology investments.

Adobe emphasized that the expansion isn’t intended to give AI agents unrestricted authority over creative decisions. “CX Enterprise Coworker enables human-led agent-accelerated work,” Parmar said. “At every step when the agent is making a decision, it’s coming back to the human and asking.”

Early deployments have accelerated audience definition, traditionally a time-consuming part of campaign development, according to Adobe, although executives provided no quantitative productivity figures. The company expects similar improvements in content production.

The new GenStudio integrations are being made available today, with some Firefly Graph capabilities remaining in beta test. Adobe said the expanded Coworker functionality won’t require additional charges for customers with existing subscriptions.

New Bilt Deals With Hyatt, Marriott And Sports Team Reveal Bigger Bet On Rewards Across Your Whole Neighborhood

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Bilt wants the building you live in, the hotel program you belong to, and the team you root for to get you recognized and rewarded at businesses around you. Its new Neighborhood OS and membership platform bring together merchants, housing communities, Hyatt, Marriott and professional sports teams in a much bigger play than earning points on rent. The ambition is to become the company that helps businesses find their next customer, gives that customer a reason to do business, and makes the experience good enough that they come back. I’ve been writing about Bilt’s distribution advantage since 2021. They figured out how to reach customers everyone else wanted but failed to reach – through their homes. They’ve since built what I consider the most valuable points currency and the best rewards for my everyday credit card spending. Now they’re opening up the technology and merchant network behind those rewards for other companies to use. There are two pieces to the announcement: Tools for merchants. Neighborhood OS connects the reservation, payment, point of sale and loyalty systems a business already uses. The goal is to help it recognize customers, deliver benefits, bring in new business and encourage repeat visits. Loyalty programs for other organizations. Bilt is making its membership platform available under other companies’ brands. A sports team, hotel company or residential community can build a new membership program, or extend its existing one, with benefits at businesses around it. The partners include the Phoenix Suns, Houston Rockets, Houston Texans and Sacramento Kings; Marriott and Hyatt; and real estate companies including Related and Presidio Bay. Broader integrations and new membership experiences will roll out in phases beginning in 2027. Bilt CEO Ankur Jain describes a $6.6 trillion opportunity in annual U.S. retail and food services spending that takes place in person. That’s the size of the market they’re aiming at. Small goals! “Your neighborhood is your most important amenity.” — Ankur Jain, introducing Bilt Neighborhood OS What originally interested me in Bilt was the way they acquired customers. An airline already has people who want its miles sitting on its planes. A new, independent rewards program has to find consumers, persuade them to join, and give them a reason to stick around. That’s tough, but Bilt had a truly original angle in reaching people through their homes, rewarding them for paying rent. Bilt helped solve billing and resident relationship problems for landlords, and found a way to become focal for customers. As I wrote earlier this year, move-in is an especially valuable moment: someone is choosing a new coffee shop, gym, dry cleaner and place to order dinner. Their habits are up for grabs. The company says it has signed one in four U.S. apartment buildings and expects to process more than $100 billion in housing payments this year. Their customers are far broader than the small minority of Bilt members with one of their credit cards. They’re equally happy for you to participate with whatever cards you already carry. We’ve seen attempts to create a common rewards program across different merchants before. American Express launched Plenti in 2015 with companies including AT&T, ExxonMobil, Macy’s and Rite Aid. It shut down in July 2018. Bilt’s chairman Ken Chenault was running American Express when Plenti launched. Plenti had enormous merchants with plenty of customers, but struggled to create was a compelling new relationship that made those customers change where they spent. The tech wasn’t very good, they didn’t broaden out merchants, and the value proposition was more modest. Coalition programs have a much longer history abroad. Canada’s Air Miles built a huge membership, although its financial troubles led to BMO acquiring it in 2023. Britain’s Nectar has 24 million active users. The U.S. still hasn’t produced a modern coalition on the scale Plenti promised. Bilt, though, offers a better way to reach customers and a more leveraged rewards system. A small discount is worth its face value. Points that can become a great hotel stay or an international business class award can be worth a lot more to the member than they cost to deliver. And Bilt’s partnerships work together to provide better experiences (e.g. a discount, together with a free Lyft ride to the restaurant, and a comped dessert). And their scale gives local merchants a much more compelling rewards program than they could have on their own. A customer might never visit one restaurant often enough to care about its standalone program. But their rewards can pool with others in the community to create something customers value. And they can reach the customers of those other local businesses, too. That restaurant can identify someone as a customer to solicit and a potentially valuable client to treat especially well. So for merchants: Find customers who can become regulars. A restaurant with empty tables on Tuesday can reach people in nearby buildings who are deciding where to eat. Spend the reward budget where it helps. An offer can be aimed at a first visit, a quiet evening or someone who hasn’t returned recently without giving away half the bill to everyone on a Saturday night when the restaurant is already full. Connect the visit to the relationship. Reservations, purchases and loyalty information can help the business recognize a customer, remember preferences and follow up. A good owner does this for the regulars they know, but the software can help scale it for more people. Offer something customers value more than it costs. A complimentary appetizer, a preferred table when available, or access to an otherwise empty fitness class can make a customer feel appreciated without requiring an equivalent cash discount. There’s a second distribution advantage here. Bilt is working with Verifone, Fiserv, Adyen and OpenTable. Those companies already have relationships with merchants and systems inside their businesses. Connecting to that installed base makes expansion much easier than persuading every restaurant to replace its payment terminal and reservation software. Bilt’s tech is better than what others have tried in the past. Here’s Bilt’s illustration of the merchant experience: The artificial intelligence component isn’t just buzz marketing. They can handle requests and coordinate actions across systems that otherwise require staff to do the work manually. Being able to identify the audience, make an offer, apply it correctly and help the merchant understand what happened is more useful. And Bilt says 91% of actions are completed without manual intervention. I’m not sure if that’s really good, or if a 9% failure rate is unacceptably high at scale. But it’ll clearly improve quickly. Bilt’s merchant sales page lists a $495 monthly Neighborhood OS subscription and an optional $195 monthly Neighborhood Marketplace subscription, or $690 for both. It lists a $1,500 onboarding fee waived through December 31, 2026. Bilt says members generated more than $12 billion in rewarded commerce at local merchants over the last year. A hotel company has a relationship with a guest while they’re traveling, but the guest spends money at restaurants and shops outside the hotel. A sports team has fans all year, including days when there’s no game. Those are valuable relationships with plenty of spending happening outside the company’s own business. Sports fans. The Phoenix Suns and Phoenix Mercury plan to launch in spring 2027 inside their existing app including concession offers, the ability to purchase seat upgrades when inventory opens, and neighborhood benefits connected to team wins. A fan concierge could help with a ride after the game or a restaurant offer nearby. Hotel loyalty. Hyatt and Bilt say they’re expanding incentives and exclusive offers at Hyatt properties, and exploring ways to reward World of Hyatt members through neighborhood experiences. Marriott is also named as a partner. Delivery and dining. Grubhub’s partnership is designed to connect delivery and restaurant visits. A restaurant could encourage someone who orders at home to come in for dinner, or give a diner a reason to order delivery later, through connected campaigns. Bilt announced a $300 million acquisition of LAVA last week, bringing relationships with 56 professional sports franchises and more than 300 technology adapters. That buys both access to fan communities and connections to the systems those teams use. Bilt calls its consumer pass Neighborhood Pass and has an identity system that lets businesses recognize eligible customers across systems without exchanging raw customer data. Getting the benefit applied correctly is a big part of the value. Nobody wants to explain a promotion to a waiter who’s never heard of it. In contrast, one senior executive who sought my counsel on whether to work with Plenti a decade ago concluded it was a non-starter that customers were going to have to submit individual receipts to the program for credit. I already get value from Bilt that goes well beyond a points balance. I’ve redeemed Bilt Cash this year for two BLADE flights, Blacklane and hotel discounts, and use benefits with Lyft, Grubhub and Walgreens. The opportunity here is to make useful benefits available across many more of the places people already spend time. Bilt already earns merchant commissions for sending spending to partners. Subscription fees add another revenue stream. Aggregating many small businesses gives it something valuable to offer a national hotel company, bank or sports sponsor. Bringing those companies’ customers and budgets to local merchants makes joining more attractive. They raised $250 million at a $10.75 billion valuation in July 2025. That provides resources to build and buy the technology, sign partners and support expansion. I think they have a real shot, but what they are doing is really hard. But each piece they successfully build makes subsequent pieces more valuable. A restaurant makes the network more useful to residents nearby. Those residents make it more attractive to the next merchant. A competitor has to assemble both sides in the same places. Two sided markets are hard! Bilt, which launched publicly just five years ago, has built this incredibly quickly. This is the biggest bunch of meaningless PR fluff I’ve ever read.

They want to be a middleman and they’ve done payment processing for some big landlords. Exciting. Unfortunately, their 2026 was a complete disaster – dumped by a real bank, picked up by PE who wanted pie in the sky but instead got 3 versions of a credit card in 8 months that never achieved any market penetration. On the heels of that failure they’re pulling the rug out from the only part of the credit card that offered a bit more value to consumers than they could get from reliable banks.

All that means is that the marketing dollars they put into their growth plan did not result in more growth, so the marketing dollars had to be cut off because the hockey stick growth that would have supported the charade never materialized.

Now, of course, they have PE investors that would like to make some money off of this failure. So what do they do? They have some digital infrastructure that is underutilized and that they’d like to more broadly deploy. So back to basics… meaningless puffery and branding! They’re trying to refocus on the “meh” stuff that they originally did, because when pie in the sky fails, that’s what you do. Notably absent from this announcement though are any real, meaningful partnerships for “the Neighborhood OS”. Such branding and hubris.

Anyway, good news everyone, our neighborhoods are about to be brought together like never before by Bilt’s amazing AI capabilities… you know, the ones that couldn’t even answer questions about its own program accurately? In fairness, Bilt executives couldn’t answer questions about its own program accurately, so seems harsh to blame the AI.

Enjoy the grandfathering while it lasts, folks.

We Need More Games Like Murals

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A simple game, straight from the heart of a studio that's been around the block

You don't hear much about game developers like the U.K.'s Dlala Studios: neither a AAA megalith nor a fashionable indie, but a smallish outfit that's been chipping away at the coalface for years, making characterfully animated versions of other people's properties. I suspect there are more of these studios out there than any of us realize, propping up bigger projects with their work-for-hire services in between trying to get their own games off the ground.

You might know Dlala from its 2020 take on Rare's vintage beat-em-up Battletoads, or from its 2023 Mickey Mouse platformer, Disney Illusion Island. Now it's time for the studio's "one for me," in the form of Murals, a combat-heavy isometric action-adventure that looks like Hades but is chasing something much simpler and more nostalgic: a linear cartoon romp full of wit and quirky charm.

I found Dlala in an appealingly scruffy workspace on a small industrial estate in the unremarkable commuter town of Witham in Essex, a 40-minute train ride from London. Founder and local boy Aj Grand-Scrutton (huge, chatty, barrel-chested, with the hair and beard of Gandalf) proudly gave a tour in his slides, apologizing for the absence of his dog and the number of monitors plugged into his PC (seven). He finished with the cinema projection room management had built in secret while the staff was working remotely during COVID.

For a studio like this, it's all about keeping the lights on, but Grand-Scrutton is careful not to disown Battletoads and Illusion Island, which he says the studio was given a lot of freedom on, as mere work-for-hire. He considers both "true Dlala games." Nevertheless, Murals is different. For one thing, Dlala will self-publish it. For another, it is entirely Dlala's own creation, unless you count the classic fairy tales it is extremely loosely based on.

In Dlala's offices, I played the first two chapters, or "murals," of the game, based on Sleeping Beauty and Rapunzel. The player controls The Hero, a sturdy, silent protagonist in baggy overalls who can inline-skate around for a lick of extra speed. The Hero has been transported into this twisted fairy-tale mural by The Creator, a self-aware meta-villain; in the process, The Hero's sidekick Loaf, a chirpy and loquacious slice of bread, has been accidentally brought to life from a sketch in The Hero's pocket.

Grand-Scrutton has a passion for 2D animation (he idolizes 16-bit titles like Cool Spot and Aladdin), and loves to write silly, surreal, pun-riddled scripts. The Hero picks up a sword called Slash Gordon and a medallion called Dragon Thee Dallion. Sleeping Beauty has turned from a soporific damsel into a haughty insomniac called Bri who needs to listen to heavy metal at full volume to sleep. Rapunzel is Harriet, tortured by her own hair, and attended by a surfer-dude prince called Kit who is blinded by "the foreboding weight of my previous self." Grand-Scrutton is extremely proud to have obtained the services of dadaist British comedy legend Bob Mortimer to voice Magic Dave, a vendor of boons. (If you want to laugh until you can't breathe anymore, search for clips of Mortimer's appearances on the panel show Would I Lie to You?)

In other words, we're in Whimsy Town. This will either appeal to you or it won't, but there's no doubt that Dlala is pouring all its personality into the game, as well as its distinctive art and animation style. Grand-Scrutton admits the studio is less sure of itself when it comes to combat, but says the team enjoyed the experience of making Battletoads and wanted to challenge themselves to make a combat-first game. You can tell: to begin with, fighting in Murals feels quite basic, but it quickly builds in confidence without betraying its simplicity. It's frantic and fun.

The twisted fairy-tale skits that make up Murals have been invaded by The Distorted, a breed of paint-themed creatures. (As well as Dlala's own Mickey game, Murals seems influenced by Warren Spector's Epic Mickey.) The Hero has ranged attacks, melee combos, and area-of-effect blasts which are modified by equipment and Eeries, the roguelike-style modifiers sold by Magic Dave. There's room to sculpt your own build here, and Dlala isn't too concerned if it breaks the balance.

But by far the most important button is the dodge button. The Hero's health pool is small, and Murals is much more about dodging attacks than stringing together fancy combos or executing perfect parry timing. It's rhythmic and spatial, predicated on the attack patterns of each enemy type. Murals keeps layering these on: cannon things that launch splats at your location, blobs that breed mini blobs, large cattle things that charge. Just to reinforce the value of dodging, there's a bonus for kill streaks without taking any damage.

I hope Murals works out for Dlala. The game’s combination of simple structure and strong personality aren’t as common as you might think. And while there might be more Dlalas out there than we realize, it’s probably also true that there are fewer of them every day.

PuppyGraph raises $10M to let AI agents query enterprise data as a graph

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Graph query engine startup PuppyGraph said today it has raised $10 million in new funding to build out its enterprise go-to-market operation.

The round follows the general release of version 1.0 of its software. PuppyGraph provides a query engine that lets companies treat data already sitting in their warehouses and lakehouses as a single graph, without first moving it into a separate graph database. The company says artificial intelligence agents running on it can work across 3,000 enterprise tables and get graph query results back in under a second.

Agent projects tend to hit trouble once they leave the pilot stage, according to PuppyGraph. The company’s own example asks which critical systems an attacker could reach from a compromised account, a question that means tracing how identities and permissions connect across thousands of tables in several systems.

A proof of concept might cover 30 to 50. Teams that scale past that find schema descriptions alone eating into an agent’s context window, and PuppyGraph said queries running several relationships deep can stall until they time out.

Data teams setting up PuppyGraph 1.0 start from a schema that an AI-assisted tool drafts out of their existing tables. Once they have approved it, users can put questions to the graph in plain English.

PuppyGraph has its own agent for that, and the Model Context Protocol lets companies bring in the AI tools they already use. Agents are kept from guessing how the data fits together. Any agent that references a relationship nobody set up gets its query bounced, and the error that comes back is written for the agent to act on.

When PuppyGraph connects to Snowflake Inc. or Databricks Inc. it can obtain short-lived credentials tied to the user’s identity, so nobody has to hand over long-lived keys. Row-level permissions configured on the data still apply, and the company says it checks them at each hop of a traversal.

“The insights of enterprise data will soon be generated by agents,” said Weimo Liu (pictured), co-founder and chief executive of PuppyGraph. “Users will expect to ask questions that cross departments and systems, beyond the workflows someone built into a dashboard.”

PuppyGraph’s customer list includes Advanced Micro Devices Inc. and Palo Alto Networks Inc., and the announcement also named Coinbase Global Inc., Datadog Inc. and Trend Micro Inc. as customers. Xinyu Liu, a staff software engineer at Coinbase, said that because the exchange connects straight to tables it already keeps in Snowflake and Databricks, “standing up a new graph is a schema definition, not a data migration.” Weimo Liu worked as a software engineer at Google LLC before co-founding the Santa Clara-based company.

PuppyGraph’s annual recurring revenue grew sevenfold over the past year, by the startup’s count. No dollar figure was disclosed.

Particle Future and defy.vc led the Series A round. It is the second time defy.vc has led a round for PuppyGraph, after a $5 million seed round in November 2024. PuppyGraph will also put some of the new money into engineering.

Amy Yin, a partner at defy.vc, said writing a query is now a job a user can hand to Anthropic PBC’s Claude. Executing it across billions of relationships still “takes serious engineering,” in her view.

Developers can download a free version of PuppyGraph with a single Docker command and no sign-up form, and the company says it takes about 10 minutes to go from deployment to a first query. Investors have now put $15 million into the startup.

‘Godzilla Minus Zero’ Officially Delivers Record-Breaking Box Office Debut

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

Two overseas titles secured spots on the top-five list of the domestic box office this weekend, with Godzilla Minus Zero claiming the number-one spot and Ramayana: Rise of a Legend finishing fifth. While the top-five list was bookended by the two international movies, three vastly different homegrown titles took the remaining three spots. Among them were two new releases, the animated offering The Cat in the Hat, and the period musical Drummer Boy.

Ramayana: Rise of a Legend is the most expensive Indian film of all time, with a reported production budget of approximately $200 million. The movie is the first part of a planned duology, with the second installment currently slated for a 2028 release. A grand international publicity tour was mounted to promote the mythological epic, necessitated by its massive production cost. As a rule of thumb, a film this size needs to gross twice its reported budget to break even. Ramayana got off to a lukewarm start, with $55 million globally and around $12 million domestically this past weekend.

Godzilla Minus Zero, on the other hand, had built-in buzz thanks to the positive word-of-mouth earned by its predecessor, Godzilla Minus One. Released in 2023, the previous installment grossed more than $110 million worldwide against a reported budget of $10 million. The movie broke box-office records for international titles in North America, having grossed more than $55 million in its theatrical run. Godzilla Minus One rode a wave of positive buzz to win the Best Visual Effects Oscar, beating out mega-budget behemoths.

The 2023 film was set in post-war Japan, serving as an allegory for the bombing of Hiroshima and Nagasaki. Many viewed it as a companion piece to Christopher Nolan's biographical thriller Oppenheimer, which was released the same year. Like Nolan's film, Godzilla Minus One was critically acclaimed. It now holds a near-perfect 99% score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Godzilla Minus Zero has also received positive reviews. The film is currently sitting at a "Certified Fresh" 90% critics score and a "Verified Hot" 96% audience score on the aggregator website, where the consensus reads, "Keeping its epic action rooted in human interest, Godzilla Minus Zero solidifies director Takashi Yamazaki's take on the King of Monsters as one of the mightiest yet by upping the ante without losing sight of its lovable characters." Buoyed by positive reviews, the film grossed nearly $32 million in its domestic debut, nearly tripling the $11 million opening-weekend haul of its predecessor.

This is also the biggest debut for an international live-action movie. Godzilla Minus Zero's global opening stood at $63 million against a reported budget of $15 million. Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

‘Sonic the Hedgehog’ Officially Returns With New Release

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

There was always one kid who got stuck playing Tails in Sonic the Hedgehog 2. If your friend had the first controller, you spent half of Emerald Hill trying to keep up and the other half waiting for Sonic to stop long enough for you to catch up. Still, when Sega introduced Miles “Tails” Prower in the 1992 Genesis game, handing somebody that second controller meant they could actually join you for the ride.

Thirty years later, the movie sequel with the same name gave the two their own big-screen team-up. Colleen O’Shaughnessey carried her role as Tails from the games into the movies, where the fox joined Ben Schwartz’s Sonic in taking on Dr. Robotnik. For fans who met the pair on the Genesis, though, a couple of red shoes and two wildly different patches of terrain are enough to place them.

Collider can exclusively share details on Dark Horse and Sega’s new classic Sonic and Tails vinyl figures, which pull the pair straight out of the 1992 game. Previously teased at San Diego Comic-Con, the figures kick off Dark Horse’s official Sonic the Hedgehog collectible line. Bigshot Toyworks sculpted the figures, with J.W. Productions handling prototyping and paint.

Four of the largest communities on the planet, and four completely different reasons to belong to one. Here is yours, what it would give you, and the argument you would be having within a week.

You belong to the fandom that is really about comfort. Hogwarts works on people because it is a place rather than a plot, and the pull is not the wizard war so much as the corridors, the food, the weather and the sense that somebody is expecting you.

You belong to the deepest fandom on this list, the one where somebody has always read further than you and is quietly delighted to say so. Middle-earth rewards patience more than any other world in popular culture.

You belong to the loudest and largest tent of the four, the one where three generations are all watching the same thing and none of them agree about any of it. That disagreement is not a flaw in this fandom, it is the main activity.

You belong to the thinking fandom, the one that turns up for the argument rather than the explosion. Trek has always been a series of ethical problems in space, and the people who love it love being asked what they would have done.

Sonic stands 8 inches tall, with one of his red shoes kicked back and a cloud of dust trailing him as he tears across a Chemical Plant Zone base. If you spent your childhood dreading the part of Chemical Plant where the pink liquid starts rising, there’s something funny about seeing Sonic look this unbothered. He’s already halfway out of there.

Tails gets a piece of Emerald Hill Zone, the opening stage of the game that introduced him. His figure stands 7 inches tall and catches him on foot just before takeoff, with both tails raised behind him and his red-and-white shoes still on the ground. Put the two side by side and it looks like Sonic has already taken off for the next level while Tails is about to take the shortcut.

The Sonic and Tails figures retail for $59.99 each. Dark Horse is offering $10 off when you pre-order them together through Dark Horse Direct, bringing the pair to $109.98 before tax and shipping. Both figures are expected to ship in Q3 2027, while supplies last. Stay tuned to Collider for updates.

Reddit Taps Qloo To Strengthen Its Predictive Targeting

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

If someone likes a local Italian restaurant, predicting that they might like another, similar Italian restaurant isn’t exactly rocket science (or, should we say, data science).

What’s a bit more impressive is figuring out that the same person might like a certain fashion brand and have a proclivity for jazz music because they’re looking at a discussion thread about upscale pizza restaurants. Those are the sort of findings that Reddit is hoping to uncover with its new partnership with predictive inference company Qloo (pronounced “clue”).

The partnership, announced on Tuesday, will provide Reddit with access to over 10 years of Qloo’s proprietary data, helping it serve more relevant ads to users.

In 2019, Qloo acquired TasteDive, a multi-industry review and recommendation platform. The data it collects through TasteDive is a key part of its catalogue, Qloo CEO and Founder Alex Elias told AdExchanger. The catalogue tracks correlations between different interests (or “entities”), like restaurants and musical artists, based on shared attributes.

Qloo determines similarities by categorizing entities based on a range of data points from sources, including TasteDive reviews, metadata from the open-source knowledge base Wikidata and data licensed from third parties.

For instance, the famous Italian restaurant in New York I Sodi isn’t just categorized as “Italian food,” Elias said. Rather, Qloo’s knowledge base also pays attention to signals that describe its architecture and ambiance (which Elias described as “Scandinavian minimalism”) so it can be linked to, say, fashion brands with a similar aesthetic – not just comparable Italian restaurants.

Understanding the shared attributes that wouldn’t be obvious at first glance is one of the main reasons Reddit has elected to partner with Qloo, said Virgilio Pigliucci, Reddit’s senior director of engineering.

Users can opt into personalized ads on Reddit, which are particularly helpful when a user is scrolling through their personal feed and engaging with a variety of content, said Pigliucci. But when someone is on a particular subreddit, Reddit generally aims to serve them ads that are “contextually similar” to the posts they’re looking at, he added.

Qloo’s data helps Reddit find connections between products that might not seem to have much in common.

The signals that Reddit gets from its own out-of-the-box data usually suggest advertising a product that is directly related to the post a user is reading, said Pigliucci, like an espresso machine ad on a post about the best coffeemakers.

Using Qloo’s data, Reddit saw that people who are in the market for an espresso machine are also often looking to buy a table that the machine can live on. Now, Reddit might serve an ad for a tall desk on that same thread, rather than echoing recommendations that are already organically cropping up in the conversation.

Historically, it could take days or weeks for Reddit to optimize a new advertiser’s campaign because it needed to track the way users engaged with the ads, Pigliucci said. Now, Reddit has a better understanding of connections between different categories and products and can “optimize against the signals” provided by Qloo by finding a close proxy to the advertiser or ingesting its prior data – no user engagement data necessary.

Plus, Qloo’s data allows for more resonant advertising without requiring any personal data, Pigliucci said. The model draws correlations between entities without having to know the user’s search query, browsing history or even what subreddit they’re in.

It’s a win-win for advertisers and clients. Client privacy is protected, and advertisers see results “right away,” said Pigliucci, “instead of having to wait a little bit,” as was the standard with its previous machine learning capabilities.

No major retail media player has taken as circumspect an approach to ad revenue as Shopify. But the company has recently taken its next important step with a product called Campaign Autopilot, VP of product for merchant marketing Samir Pradhan tells AdExchanger.

OpenAI’s head of monetization, Benji Shomair makes the case for ads as a way to bring ChatGPT to more people, with user trust taking priority over advertiser demands.

In Q3, The Trade Desk beat the admittedly pessimistic revenue forecast it set for itself earlier this year. But growth has stalled.

Viant is doubling down on CTV with its $20 million Waymark acquisition, betting that AI-generated ads can bring more advertisers to streaming TV.

TIME is using AI agents to draft proposals and troubleshoot campaigns – but not to replace the human relationships that drive ad sales.

Why the midterms may not spell doom for ad tech consolidation. Plus, how sports leagues are putting ad revenue before player safety.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

Cashmere Perfumes Are the Coziest Way to Wear Fragrance

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

These soft, musky scents capture the plush warmth of your favorite sweater.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Few things are as cozy as cashmere—whether it’s a sweater, scarf, or throw blanket. One unexpected way to wrap yourself in the fabric’s luxury is with a cashmere perfume. These scents capture the best parts of the warm, fuzzy material through an actual cashmere note called cashmeran. The synthetic note, used in perfume since it was developed in the 1960s, adds warmth, woodiness, musk, and spice to a perfume’s layers, giving the overall composition a soft, velvety texture. Grounded in the warm woods and sweet gourmands of the season, or anchored alongside a fresh floral, these cashmere perfumes are the next best thing to a premium garment.

The richness of sandalwood and the creaminess of a milk accord layer seamlessly at the bottom and top of a cashmeran center.

Key notes: milky accord, nutmeg, Iris, rose, cashmeran, sandalwood, Madagascar vanilla bourbon

Customer review: “Santal Calling is divine; on my skin it is magic. Pure comfort in a bottle—creamy sandalwood wrapped in soft iris, warm milk, and the smoothest touch of vanilla. It feels like cashmere against the skin: elegant, intimate, and so addictive that I can't stop spraying myself throughout the day.” - Madrone

Many lavender perfumes capture the essence of the flower itself, but Matiere Premiere took a different approach, encapsulating the atmospheric notes from a lavender distillation factory in Provence. The result is a hybrid of fresh lavender blending with crisper hints of metal that are then softened by musk and cashmeran.

Customer review: “For me, lavender is a tricky scent. It depends how it is paired in a perfume composition. This one opens with a very "fresh" lavender, which will go quiet in the background, and it will reappear in the dry down. Lovely perfume, easy to wear and very good longevity (6+ hours on my skin).” - Ramona D.

One of Maison Margiela’s most popular scents blends the smokiness of burning wood with notes of gaiac wood, cashmeran, and clove oil to bottle the experience of sitting by a crackling fireplace.

Key notes: clove oil, chestnut accord, vanilla accord

Customer review: “My all-time favorite perfume. It smells like sitting by the fireplace wrapped in your comfy blanket, sipping your favorite drink. The scent is perfect for winter!” - MelB

This Mugler classic intensifies as it warms up with your body heat, bringing out the long-lasting notes of white amber, cashmeran wood, and jasmine. It’s equal parts floral and woody, with just the right warmth and sweetness from its amber base.

Key notes: jasmine sambac, cashmeran wood, white amber

Customer review: “This perfume has so many layers and is so sophisticated and wonderful. I really like the notes and how they mature over time. Great longevity as well.” - prespri_7178

Cashmere is the star here, anchoring the scent’s base and owning the dry-down, as toasted coconut, passionflower, and vanilla milk musk notes layer at its heart and on top.

Key notes: vanilla milk musk, toasted coconut, passionflower, plumeria, cashmeran wood

Customer review: “Soft, cozy, sweet, and luxurious. It’s giving cashmere sweater + clean skin! It’s an easy reach that I can’t stop wearing.” - Regina

This intensified version keeps the crisp, linen-like freshness of Byredo’s original Blanche eau de perfume. It takes a warmer turn as black pepper and neroli make way for a floral center, before settling into a base of white musk, cashmere wood, and ambroxan. It wears like a skin scent, although it’s in the floral family.

Key notes: aldehyde, black pepper, neroli, rose absolute, violet, jasmine, cashmeran, musks, ambroxan, cashmere woods

Customer review: “I absolutely love Blanche Absolu, intense aldehydes, clean laundry done to perfection with good longevity on skin and fabric! 10/10” - layla88

Carmina combines sweet and spicy top notes of pink pepper, black cherry, and saffron with a hearty floral center of rose, violet, and peony. To round out the profile, cashmere wood, amber, and musk peak through on the dry down.

Key notes: pink pepper, black cherry, saffron, rose de mai, violet, peony

Customer review: “It's giving 'date night,' to me. It's soft, feminine, and sensual all at the same time. As far as longevity, I sprayed it around 5pm, and I could still smell it on my wrist the next morning.”

Although it’s not quite a gourmand, Esplendor has a sweetness that’s not cloying. Bottom notes of amber and vanilla sit alongside cashmeran. Adding to the warmth at the center is benzoin, a creamy note with a balsamic-like aroma. Classic florals like rose and lily of the valley are stacked at the top to open the scent.

Key notes: lily, freesia, peony, rose, muguet, benzoin, vanilla, amber, cashmeran

Customer review: “Grandano Esplendor drapes the skin in quiet luxury. It is a cashmere sweater, washed in lilies and creamy vanilla, then folded into warm benzoin resin. The sweetness is gentle rather than sugary, and the amber gives it a comforting warmth. It doesn't overly project and lasts 8 hours, at least, on skin. It feels like velvet brushed in slow motion.” - AlchemyAge

Gap’s Om features cashmeran, musk, and amber woods—the trifecta that gives cashmere perfumes their warmth. As the scent develops, hints of spice shine through from coriander flower at the center and ginger to open the blend. Heavier notes of frankincense are balanced with light florals like water lotus.

Key notes: ginger, olibanum essence, water lotus, lilyflor, sandalwood, coriander flower, amber woods, cashmeran, cedar, balsamic musks

Customer review: “If you loved Heaven in the 90s, try the new 2026 "Om". It's smell reminds me of the old Heaven and I LOVE it. It smells like fresh laundry, clean baby clothes, spring flowers, all the calming things. I think I found a new signature scent!” - Samantha H.

Chloé’s Parfum Intense takes you on a journey that hits all the high points of a versatile fragrance. It opens with the sweetness of juicy raspberry and rose, which then shifts into a honeyed element at the center before settling into its intense ending with rich musks and woods.

Key notes: raspberry, rose, ambrox, cashmeran, cedar

Customer review: “Chloé Eau de Parfum is a wardrobe essential for anyone who treasures floral elegance that never feels fussy. It’s instantly recognizable yet versatile — perfect for spring and summer, but cozy enough for year-round wear.” -Beautybae01

Unlike many fragrances on this list that place cashmeran at the base of their composition, this one nestles it right at its heart. The scent experience begins with a kick thanks to a sweet and spicy mix of blood orange, Sichuan pepper, and ginger. You’re then brought right into a cozy heart of cashmeran and a brand-exclusive floral accord before rounding out the journey with a blend of woods, musk, and vetiver.

Key notes: ambrette, baies rose, blood orange, bergamot, Sichuan pepper, fresh ginger, fleuriste fusion, magnolia aroma space, Egyptian jasmine, cashmeran, guaiacwood, cedarwood, blond wood, vetiver, moss, ambroxan, musks

Customer review: “It’s a clean fragrance. Quiet luxury, skin scent that still has a silage that draws attention. Easy wear if you like musk. It’s all you really get. But it’s what I wanted.” - Edward B.

Donna Karan’s Cashmere Mist is quite possibly the perfume that introduced plush, cozy, cashmere-like notes to fragrance. The iconic eau de parfum, which launched in the mid-'90s, features rich florals and sandalwood—and manages to encapsulate all the soft attributes of cashmere without including cashmere wood in its composition.

Key notes: lily of the valley, Moroccan jasmine, sandalwood

Customer review: “It is a soft, sensual scent that has musky overtones. It stays with you all day long, whereas most scents disappear shortly after applying. This is one of the most pleasing scents that works with all occasions and all the different lotions I like to use. I believe I have found a scent that I will purchase over and over again without it getting old or boring. I have had numerous compliments when I wear this scent, which always makes a girl happy.” - Sasicat

Area’s Toms Collaboration Is Made for the After-Party

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Designer Nicholas Aburn on bringing the brand’s signature sparkle to the humble Toms Alpargata.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

“I love designing a big look,” says Nicholas Aburn, the creative director of the maximalist, party-ready brand Area. “But there’s something to be said for making special things that people can actually wear. I want to make the dress that you had the best night in, that you got laid in, that you went home with your partner for the first time in. It has to be fun and special, but also it has to be normal enough that you actually wear it.”

Aburn, who worked at Demna’s Balenciaga and is now in his second year at Area, carried that idea into the brand’s spring/summer 2027 collection. Amid the razzle-dazzle—crystal-covered looks, sculptural silhouettes, and metallic heels that announced themselves with sound—came a surprise: Toms. The humble shoe, part of a limited-edition collaboration available now, received an Area-sized makeover, with the Alpargata style reimagined through the label’s party-inflected lens.

The limited-edition collection recasts Toms’s cult silhouette in five variations. The Crystal Slit, available in white and black colorways, riffs on Area’s celebrity-favorite jeans of the same name, with crystals diagonally tracing the vamp. Balletcore gets two takes in the ballet flat: one with a statement crystal shoelace bow, the other covered in tonal rhinestones. There’s also something on offer for the minimalist: the Sculpture Alpargata, which features origami-like folds. Toms’s molded footbed and plush insoles lend themselves to all-day (and night) comfort, while the embellishments bring an intentional after-party sparkle.

Aburn is often approached by brands about collaborations, but Toms was an easy yes, even if the two might seem worlds apart. “I think even Toms was surprised that I was up for it,” the designer said. “Some of my colleagues were like, ‘That’s the one you are willing to pull the trigger on?’ But Toms is iconic, it’s American, and I like that it comes from this very casual place.”

That easygoing spirit came through on the runway. The shoes were styled with some of the collection’s more relaxed pieces: ink-splattered shorts, a draped cowl-neck top, and pooling white jeans with feather trim. It’s how Aburn envisions them being worn in real life. “At weddings, everyone takes off their heels and puts on those pass-out flip-flops,” he explains. “I like the idea that you bring the Toms in your bag and throw them on when you’re tired. You still have your dress on, your hair is kind of messy. Everything gets a little cooler at the end of the night.”

Aburn, like many, associates Toms with the “indie sleaze” era, when they were ubiquitous with skinny jeans. (He bought his first pair after seeing Mary-Kate and Ashley Olsen wear them.) Now, with Area’s signature embellishments in the mix, Aburn already has a few dream wearers in mind.

“[The] Sculpture, which doesn’t actually have sparkles on it, I would be very excited to see guys in. I’d love to see Jacob Elordi in those. Taylor Swift has been a big supporter of the brand; she’d look great in the Crystal Slit ones. And I’d love to see some cool downtown girl like Ivy Wolk wearing the ballet flats.”

It Will Become Easier and Easier to Regrow Your Hair

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

With new technology, hair loss might be a thing of the past.

As part of the Future of Beauty Awards, the ELLE beauty team looked ahead at what will be next in beauty and wellness.

It’s been a trying decade: A pandemic, political turmoil, and everyday stressors are enough to make anyone’s hair fall out, and it has. Nearly 30 million women in the United States experience pattern hair loss, according to the Cleveland Clinic. Add on the potential of GLP-1-related hair loss, and we seem to be in an endless cycle of shedding. But could we finally be approaching a way to cure it? New in-office injectables and prescription drugs are poised to change the game for good.

Newly available at dermatologists’ offices, Xvie is a multi-signal, exosome-rich concentrate made from amniotic fluid. Exosomes are tiny vesicles released by cells; they contain proteins, lipids, and nucleic acids. “Xvie takes a multi-signal approach to hair thinning by supporting cellular communication,” says Trillitye Paullin, PhD, a founding member and chief scientific officer of Xvie. Amy B. Lewis, MD, a dermatologist in New York who uses Xvie clinically, says she has seen it work its magic in real time and uses a magnifying camera to show patients where it has helped encourage new growth—which she calls a “quantitative result,” since they’re able to count new follicle growth. Xvie is currently in trials as an injectable, but right now, doctors can apply it topically on the scalp after microneedling to bolster hair growth.

One topical drug, PP405, currently in clinical trials has doctors excited for its ability to wake up dormant stem cells in hair follicles. A Phase 2 trial done with UCLA found that patients who applied the molecule to their scalps for four weeks didn’t just get peach fuzz—they experienced “terminal hair” growth, meaning full shafts that have the potential to be long and thick. “Importantly, it regrew hair even on bald scalps,” says Marc R. Avram, MD, a clinical professor of dermatology at Weill Cornell Medical College specializing in hair loss. “That’s against dogma.” While the molecule still needs to go through Phase 3 trials, Avram is hopeful that it could be available for patients within two years. Once it’s approved, it will likely be a great option for reversing female- or male-pattern baldness (otherwise known as androgenetic alopecia).

PP405 isn’t the only molecule with potential. Flourish Biologics is developing a patented technology based on the discovery that the parathyroid hormone—which binds to receptors in the body to control calcium levels in the blood—also contributes to hair growth. Its injectable targets androgenetic alopecia. Right now, it’s only being tested on animals, but David Leffell, MD, an emeritus dermatological surgeon at Yale and the chief executive officer of Flourish Biologics, thinks that a shot for humans is on the horizon. “While early animal tests have been strong, there are a number of steps before the human trials, which will probably occur in 12 to 18 months,” he says.

Doctors are also excited by a new form of minoxidil. “It works even better,” confirms Avram, based on data from ongoing clinical trials, though he notes that peer review and approval are still pending. Everyone has heard of Rogaine, a topical form of the drug. (Cue Aidan snatching his from Carrie’s hands in Sex and the City and snarling, “It’s preventative!”) The current version of oral minoxidil spikes quickly in the bloodstream, which may cause dizziness and heart palpitations—and while it does work, it isn’t the ideal concentration for sustained hair growth, according to a paper published in Dermatology Times. The new extended-release form has been shown to stay at the ideal dosage for hair growth over the course of 12 hours, with less risk of cardiac side effects. Biopharmaceutical company Veradermics finished a Phase 2 trial with encouraging results, and a long-acting oral minoxidil pill might be available as early as 2027. But for anyone worried about hair loss, it’s worth talking to a doctor now. “We’re in an era where if you seek treatment early, you can keep a good head of hair on your head,” Avram says.

A version of this story appears in the November 2026 issue of ELLE.

How to Treat Acne Like an Adult

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Adult acne suuucks. We’re here to help you treat it (no Stridex pads in sight).

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

EVERYONE KNOWS NOT to squeeze a pimple, so what’s a grown-ass man supposed to do to clear his acne? Consider this your official guide to treating breakouts at home or with the help of a dermatologist.

Below are the most effective ingredients available in over-the-counter (OTC) acne products. Use ’em daily to keep breakouts at bay and combine them for the best results…provided your skin doesn’t freak out. Whether you use an ingredient in a cleanser, toner, serum, moisturizer, or as a targeted spot treatment depends on what your skin can handle.

As a beta-hydroxy acid (BHA), salicylic acid gently exfoliates dead skin cells and dissolves excess sebum that can clog pores, says Joshua Zeichner, MD, a dermatologist at Mount Sinai Hospital. It can be effective in facial cleansers, toners, serums, moisturizers, or spot treatments, but a body wash is ideal for larger areas like the back or chest. You’ll spy the ingredient in 0.5% to 2% concentrations, though the latter is considered most effective for acne-prone skin.

BP is a winner because it “lowers the levels of acne-causing bacteria and has some anti-inflammatory effects,” Dr. Zeichner explains. It can be irritating, so use a lower percentage (2.5% to 10% is the FDA-approved range) for a leave-on product, and a higher concentration for a rinse-off product.

Try: Neutrogena Evenly Clear Acne Cleansing Mask

No, you won’t smell like rotten eggs—and if you think of yourself as the sensitive type, sulfur (at 3% to 10%) is a solid, nonirritating option that absorbs oil, inhibits bacteria growth, calms redness, and mildly exfoliates. It’s a popular ingredient in spot-treatment products and face masks.

Best For: Blackheads, whiteheads, and mild red bumps on sensitive skin

Once available only by prescription, adapalene is a dermatologist favorite, even at its 0.1% OTC concentration. Like other topical retinoids such as retinol, which speed skin cell turnover and stimulate collagen production, it also has anti-inflammatory properties. (Bonus: As a vitamin A derivative, it can help reduce fine lines and wrinkles too.)

Best For: All types of acne on mature skin

Of all the alpha-hydroxy acids (AHAs) out there, glycolic acid can be a great option for someone who is acne prone, says Dr. Zeichner. Much like salicylic acid, glycolic acid removes dead skin cells and loosens excess sebum to de-gunk pores. Unlike salicylic acid (and other BHAs), it doesn’t get as deep into the pores. AHAs work primarily on the outer layers of the skin.

Try: Obagi Medical Clenziderm M.D. Resurfacing Multi-Acid Peel Pads

Severe acne like deep, painful nodules and cysts will almost always require help from a dermatologist. Depending on the type and severity of your breakouts, these are some of the most common solutions, which you’d pair with a simple at-home skin care routine (cleanser, moisturizer, and sunscreen).

A commonly prescribed topical Rx for comedonal acne (whiteheads and blackheads), this ingredient is a more potent vitamin A derivative than adapalene and could cause irritation. Most derms recommend starting slowly with a pea-size amount for your entire face once or twice a week and then gradually working up to nightly use.

Another first-line treatment are meds like doxycycline or minocycline, says dermatologist Kavita Mariwalla, MD. “These work by reducing bacterial counts in the pilosebaceous glands and are anti-inflammatory,” she explains. But they’re not a permanent solution: Dr. Mariwalla says most derms will keep patients on antibiotics temporarily (she reevaluates every eight weeks) until they begin to see results from their topical treatments (she often pairs antibiotics with topical tretinoin), which can take up to three months.

After antibiotics, this med (also known as Accutane) is the “next step up in the acne treatment pyramid,” says Dr. Mariwalla. It works by shrinking oil glands to reduce the likelihood of pore clogs that lead to acne. The most common side effects are dry skin, chapped lips, and dry eye. More serious issues, including a risk of birth defects and depression and anxiety, means your doctor will likely monitor you closely if you go this route. To mitigate these side effects, your doc may start you on a lower dose than what your weight would typically dictate—commonly known on TikTok as “microdosing Accutane.”

Spironolactone is one of the most common oral forms of these androgen-blocking medications and is typically reserved exclusively for women. (It can cause decreased libido and breast enlargement in men.) But Dr. Mariwalla says she’ll sometimes prescribe a topical version—another active called clascoterone, which goes by the brand name Winlevi—to treat red, inflamed bumps. It slows the skin’s natural oil production to help keep pimples from forming.

This oil-quelling FDA-cleared laser treatment—the first of its kind—offers an alternative to Accutane. It uses a precise wavelength of laser light to target sebum-producing glands. While effective, it can be pricey (up to $1,000 per treatment), requires several sessions, and may require maintenance treatments, Dr. Mariwalla says.

If you’ve got a whopper of a zit (a deep cyst or nodule) and a date or a big meeting to attend, dermatologists will sometimes inject cortisone to calm inflammation and quiet redness in a hurry. It’s important to note, however, that “this is a bandage, not a treatment for your acne,” says Dr. Mariwalla. “It’s effective for cysts but not acne that’s already been picked at.” If you don’t want to pay up to a couple hundred bucks for a shot (depending on your insurance), see the “Pimple Emergencies” box below.

Pimple Emergencies: Don’t squeeze! Apply a warm, damp washcloth for 10 to 15 minutes to help bring the pimple to the surface. Then apply a spot treatment with any of the OTC ingredients above. Do this daily until the pimple heals. If you’re not home, try rubbing some ice on it in 30-second intervals, then, if needed, apply a dash of 1% hydrocortisone cream to help with swelling and redness. (Pro tip: Keep this cream in your work bag to use sparingly and for no more than two to three days.) After, throw on a hydrocolloid pimple patch to aid healing or concealer to cover it.

The Guy’s Guide to Adult Acne

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

We’re all stressed AF. The food we’re eating probably isn’t helping. Everyone told us we wouldn’t have to worry about our hormones going crazy after our teen years. What the heck is going on?

THE YEAR WAS 1997. I’d just made the varsity baseball team as a freshman, and I was ready to shoot my shot with every senior girl who’d give me the time of day. The only problem: I had also hard-launched my pepperoni face. I’m not talking surface-level New York slice stuff—these breakouts were Detroit-style-pizza deep. Every clogged pore had clocked in at the same time, and I was an absolute wreck.

For six months, I’d burned through everything the CVS aisles had to offer: Oxy Pads, Stridex, Noxzema, whatever stuff was hawking a promise of clear skin. Nothing worked. My face just got redder, drier, and more irritated. My mom (thank god for her) dragged me to the dermatologist, and I walked out with a prescription for isotretinoin, a.k.a. Accutane. Five months later, my skin was completely clear. My mini CVS stash went into the trash, and I thought that’d be the end of it.

I was wrong. I spent years assuming that because I took Accutane as a teenager, I’d paid my dues, but as a 44-year-old dude, I still get the occasional pimple or two—seemingly right before a big work meeting or whenever I have a lot of stuff on my plate. And it still messes with my head. Does my hygiene suck? Are my hormones to blame? Am I sweating too much? Apparently, I’m not the only one.

According to Christopher Bunick, MD, PhD, an associate professor of dermatology at the Yale School of Medicine, 83 percent of teen boys will deal with acne before they reach their 20s, which is often due to a surge in testosterone at the time. And while acne typically clears up as testosterone levels stabilize in our 20s, it can make a surprise comeback in our 30s, 40s, 50s, and beyond—with around 20 percent of men in their 30s dealing with breakouts, but only 12 percent seeking treatment from a medical professional. It’s not just a skin issue: Research shows that adult acne is consistently linked to a higher risk of developing anxiety, depression, and reduced self-esteem.

Acne (not to be mistaken for folliculitis, which can result from shaving) is mainly caused by a combination of excess oil production and dead skin cells that clog pores. This can create an environment where strains of Cutibacterium acnes—the bacteria naturally found on your skin—become imbalanced and trigger inflammation. This could look like blackheads and whiteheads, raised pustules and papules, or the deeper, more painful nodules or cysts that are more likely to scar. And despite what your instincts tell you, excessively scrubbing your face won’t help—it could actually harm your skin barrier. “You cannot wash away hormones,” says Dr. Bunick. Acne is an inflammatory condition, not a hygiene problem.

Scientists still aren’t sure exactly what makes some guys more susceptible to acne than others, but genetics and hormones are two leading factors. Research suggests about 80 percent of your acne risk is inherited. Your genes determine how sensitive your skin is to the hormones (i.e., androgens) that regulate the sebaceous glands and how aggressively your immune system overreacts to fight the inflammation. Then there are the lifestyle factors that play a smaller role but can collectively add up, such as diet (Dr. Bunick is a believer in the gut-skin axis—the idea that an imbalanced gut microbiome can drive systemic inflammation that can predispose you to acne), stress (this spikes cortisol, which also drives oil production and contributes to inflammation), sleep deprivation (same), and testosterone replacement therapy.

Though there isn’t a cure for acne yet, there are plenty of treatment options. “Untreated acne doesn’t just affect your skin. It affects how you move through the world,” says Dr. Bunick. “Acne impacts confidence, whether it’s making friends, having a social or dating life, or performance in work, sports, or other extracurriculars.” Earlier intervention means less of a psychosocial toll, less scarring, and (you heard the man) more confidence.

So if your bathroom still looks like mine did in the ’90s, it’s time to investigate what might be causing the chaos in your largest organ and learn how to treat it. The stories below will help you get your skin clear and, hopefully, keep it that way.

Bob Gearing is a New York-based writer covering men’s grooming from the perspective of a very real (and very sweaty) dude. By day, he’s a well-SPF’d tech sales bro; by night, he’s a red-light mask guy who definitely didn’t think he’d care this much about skincare. When he’s not testing the latest face serum, you’ll find him sweating through a round of golf or holding out hope that the Lions will make the Super Bowl before he dies.

The Paths to Clear(er) Skin

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

With a little trust and patience, it’s possible to find a solution that works for you. These three guys are proof.

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

IT’S ONE THING to get advice from a doctor (still important and invaluable), but it’s another to hear from other guys who’ve been there. Guys who understand what it’s like to cancel plans because of a whopper of a zit. Guys who’ve also hesitated to take their shirts off at the pool for fear of being judged for their bacne. We wanted to hear about their struggles, and successes—what finally worked to help them send breakouts packing and regain their confidence. As you’ll learn from these three men, no two plans are the same. But with a little consistency and some experimentation, blemish-free skin is possible.

I like to take care of myself. I work out at least five times a week. I’m consistent with my diet. But my confidence takes a hit because of my acne. Since I was a teenager, I’ve dealt with deep cystic breakouts that have totally upended my life. I’ve canceled dates; I’ve canceled trips; I won’t leave the house when I have a flare. It’s improved as I’ve gotten older—since 2023 I’ve been using Winlevi, a prescription hormone-blocking cream, in the morning and at night, and Tazorac, a doctor-dispensed retinoid that I’ve been using since my 20s, at night—but I still deal with the occasional breakout. And it still messes with my confidence.

I went to Greece over the summer, and I could feel a big one coming under my skin. The one thing that really makes a difference is the La Roche-Posay Effaclar Duo+M treatment, which you can get for $25 at the drugstore. I apply it religiously every few hours as soon as I feel a zit coming on, and sometimes it helps it come to a head faster or stops it from forming altogether. I’ve found a routine that works, but acne is always something lingering in the back of my mind. —Derek Monacchio, 38, tax director

Back acne is something I’ve had my entire life, but honestly, I just thought it was something I’d always have to deal with. I couldn’t reach back there with a cream or a wash, and I didn’t want to go on Accutane or any prescription medications. Even though most people couldn’t see it, I’d get very self-conscious whenever I’d take my shirt off in public, which sucked because I take good care of my body.

That all changed when I went in for a laser hair removal appointment at a physician-led laser center, The Kaga Institute, about six months ago. The office staff knew my situation with the breakouts on my back and told me they had something that could help—a new laser called AviClear, which supposedly suppresses oil gland production. I was curious, so I gave it a try. After three sessions, the pimples on my back were gone. It was totally painless for me—especially when compared to the laser hair removal I’ve tried. Each click of the laser felt like a warm sensation for a millisecond, and I was able to continue with my laser hair removal at the same time. Now that I’ve finished my series of treatments, I was told the results should improve with time. It feels great to have found a solution that works. I’m ready for next summer already. —William Sebok, 42, construction company owner

There’s a perception that when you have acne, you find a solution and then it’s over. That has not been my experience. My first breakouts (a mix of whiteheads and inflamed red bumps) started just as I was hitting puberty, around age 11, and persisted well into my 30s. I’ve seen a few different dermatologists since I was 15 and was prescribed everything under the sun—from clindamycin (a prescription antibiotic) to Accutane. I would take the medications for a few months at a time, and some would help for a while, but once I stopped, my skin would inevitably break out again. Plus, I experienced some side effects from the Accutane, including significant lethargy.

After I stopped taking the medications, I started looking at the labels of the skin care products I was using and noticed that when the ingredient list was extremely long, there was a higher likelihood there would be something in there that would clog my pores. [Editor’s note: Look for the label “noncomedogenic,” a.k.a. not pore clogging, on your products.] For me, it became more about avoiding certain products and soothing the skin barrier. I also became much more careful about not overloading my routine with too many strong ingredients. I focused instead on making sure the products I was using were appropriate for my acne-prone skin. That was the game changer. Another thing that causes my skin to react is my diet. If I’m eating a lot of high-glycemic foods and meals that are high in sugar, I immediately break out. I also try to wash my face or shower immediately after every workout, and if I can’t, I spray on a hypochlorous acid spray to kill the bacteria. Less is more, and I’m proof of that. —Brute Choi, 37, content creator

Brian Underwood is beauty director at Women’s Health, where he oversees content strategy for the brand across all platforms, including digital, print, and social. Can Acne Be Prevented With a Vaccine?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

WHAT IF ACNE wasn’t so damn frustrating to treat or just…didn’t exist at all? George Liu, MD, PhD, and a team of researchers at the University of California, San Diego, are developing a vaccine designed to target certain types of bacteria to help prevent acne from forming in the first place.

Dr. Liu’s team researches how bacterial pathogens cause diseases. While doing so, they discovered that C. acnes—a bacteria that naturally lives on the skin and whose imbalance is a known acne trigger—secretes two forms of an enzyme called hyaluronidase: Hyl-A and Hyl-B. Hyl-A plays a central role in the development of acne by causing inflammation, whereas Hyl-B is an anti-inflammatory that can be beneficial for skin by reducing inflammation and helping prevent an aggressive immune response.

Existing acne treatments are broad and often focus on killing all C. acnes strains, which can take several months and introduce significant side effects that can range anywhere from dryness, redness, peeling, and irritation to antibiotic resistance. Instead, Dr. Liu’s vaccine—which has been tested only on rodents so far—aims to teach the immune system to block just Hyl-A, leaving the benefits of Hyl-B and preventing inflammatory acne from forming in the first place.

UC San Diego isn’t the only group chasing an acne vaccine. Paris-based pharmaceutical company Sanofi is developing an mRNA vaccine to treat active breakouts rather than prevent future ones. “The idea is to target two of the key contributors to this inflammatory cycle—the [C. acnes] bacterial strains that cause inflammation and the associated toxins—and aim to halt the disease process,” says Jean-François Toussaint, PhD, global head of vaccine research and development at Sanofi. The vaccine is currently being tested in adults with moderate to severe acne during a phase I/II clinical trial.

These vaccines could potentially do wonders for chronic acne sufferers and help save hundreds of dollars per year on acne products and medications. Between his team’s research and Sanofi’s developments, Dr. Liu predicts an acne vaccine could be available to the public within the next five years.

Sean Zucker is the Grooming and Reviews Editor at Men's Health. He brings over half a decade of experience covering health, wellness, and lifestyle. When Sean's not writing about hair products, you can find him running for exercise, or toward the nearest food truck.

Is TRT Causing Your Acne?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Guys who take higher doses of testosterone are at a greater risk of developing acne, but it can happen at any dose.

Treating adult acne can feel like one big science experiment—until now. This is the guy’s guide to adult acne. Read the rest of the stories here.

THERE ARE PLENTY of reasons to like testosterone replacement therapy (TRT)—it can boost energy, increase muscle mass, and improve sex drive—but there’s one potentially annoying side effect: acne, or, uh, bacne (acne on your back and shoulders). TRT can increase sebaceous gland activity, which boosts oil production that can clog pores, trap bacteria, and cause breakouts. The areas around the back and shoulders, along with the chest, have a high concentration of androgen-responsive oil glands.

When the total testosterone in your body increases, it gets converted to dihydro­testosterone (DHT), a major androgen, and estrogen. DHT ramps up oil production in the skin, according to Akhil Muthigi, MD, a urologist at Houston Methodist Hospital. This can lead to inflammatory acne (papules, pustules, nodules, and cysts) though whiteheads and blackheads are common as well. Dr. Muthigi says that guys who take higher doses of TRT (anything near or over 200 milligrams per week) are at a greater risk of developing acne, but it can happen at any dose. It largely depends on your skin biology (i.e., the amount of androgen receptors on your skin that trigger sebaceous gland growth and, therefore, oil production), as well as genetic factors.

TRT treatments that provide steadier levels (such as daily creams and gels) are less likely to cause acne than other forms that cause spikes (such as injections), says Danny Guo, MD, a cosmetic and surgical dermatologist. He adds that if you start TRT and see acne forming within a few weeks to a few months of taking it, stopping the therapy won’t necessarily eliminate the acne. So it’s best to talk to a dermatologist before bailing on the therapy altogether. Fortunately, TRT-triggered acne is as treatable as any form, with the strategies here proving effective.

Humans Might Have a Serious Bird Flu Problem on Their Hands

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

H5N1, otherwise known as Bird Flu, has spread across the world in 30 years, but it may be entering a deadly new era.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

Earlier this week, a resident in Yakima County in Washington State died from bird flu, a highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) virus. Known in more scientific circles as the H5N1, the virus isn’t nearly as threatening to humans as the Covid-19 coronavirus (whose first victims were similarly located in Washington State back in February 2020). But some experts are growing increasingly concerned that this influenza, which for decades has decimated avian populations (and in more recent years, severely impacted the dairy industry), could develop into something more sinister.

The first known case of H5N1 first appeared in 1996 in flocks of domestic waterfowl in southern China. By its nature as an HPAI virus, H5N1 is tailored to latch on to avian cells rather than mammalian cells, including a human’s. However, things have slowly been changing over the decades, and H5N1 mutations have made the virus much more adept at attacking mammals.

The newest threat originated during the depths of the Covid-19 pandemic as H5N1 wreaked havoc on seabirds in Europe. The virus eventually found its way to the U.S. in 2022, and hundreds of millions of birds, both wild and domestic, died as a result. A 2024 study by UC Davis reported that the virus reached the very tip of South America, infecting and killing thousands of elephant seals and leaving their bodies strewn along Argentinian beaches.

Of course, elephant seals are famously not birds, and during that same time, bovine populations in the U.S. started exhibiting signs of H5N1 infections, driven by polymerase mutations that allowed for better replication in bovine cells. Concerningly, a 2024 study demonstrated that just a single mutation to the bovine influenza H5N1, specifically the hemagglutinin (the surface protein that drives attachment), could switch the virus’s specificity to target humans.

Intermediate species can help the virus make the jump to humans (one theory suggests that this is how Covid-19 spread from bats to humans), and last month ProPublica reported that a recent outbreak of H5N1 at a mink farm in Utah is particularly distressing. Unlike those of cows or elephant seals, mink lungs and airways closely resemble ours at a cellular level, raising fears that the virus could adapt to infect humans more easily. When H5N1 impacted mink and fox farms in Finland and Spain, officials moved to cull the infected populations, killing tens of thousands of animals in the process. But ProPublica says state and federal officials in the U.S. have instead done little to curtail the spread.

“Every other country that I’m aware of that has had H5N1 in fur farms, depopulation has happened pretty quickly,” avian influenza researcher Tom Peacock told ProPublica. “The fact that the first instinct wasn’t just to depopulate and figure stuff out later is very concerning to me.”

If the worst comes to pass, the Trump Administration has gone out of its way so that the country—and the rest of the world—will be vulnerable to the virus. In May 2025, Robert F. Kennedy, the U.S. Secretary of Health of Human Service and well-known vaccine skeptic, cancelled a $700 million contract with Moderna to develop an mRNA vaccine for H5N1.

The tragic death earlier this month came from the H5N1 B.13 genotype variant seen in cows, and a CDC spokesperson speaking with Associated Press said that the virus contained no changes that would make it more contagious—but the threat very much remains.

“A bad scenario would be that someone working with cattle brought the ‘regular’ human flu in, got [H5N1] on top of that, and that develops into a mix that can spread but to which people have less immunity,” public health virologist Marion Koopmans told The Telegraph. “The chances of such an event are very low, but it is a concern shared among virologists.”

Darren lives in Portland, has a cat, and writes/edits about sci-fi and how our world works. You can find his previous stuff at Gizmodo and Paste if you look hard enough.
